+/-!
+# Length bounds for garbled messages
+
+The bounds here do not assume that encryption evaluators return correctly sized pads.
+Truncation and `zipWith` cap every ciphertext. This gives message bounds independent of
+ideal-call answers, as required by [PS10, Definition 155.1].
+
+## Main definitions
+
+This module introduces no definitions.
+
+## Main results
+
+* `length_spEncStr_le`: special encryption has at most `3 |r| + |m| + 2` bits.
+* `length_nestedSpecial_enc_le`: a nested ciphertext has at most `7 n + 4` bits.
+* `length_encoded_garbledTables_le`: the encoded tables have at most
+  `|gates| (112 n + 82)` bits for a fan-in-two circuit.
+
+## References
+
+* [PS10] R. Pass, a. shelat, *A Course in Cryptography*, lecture notes, 3rd ed.,
+  January 2010, Definition 155.1 and Protocol 162.6.
+-/
+
+namespace TCSlib.Cryptography.SecureComputation.YaoHybridProof
+
+open Complexity Turing TCSlib.Cryptography BoolCircuit
+
+/-- Special encryption with evaluator `E`, key `k`, message `m` and nonce `r` satisfies
+`|spEncStr E k m r| ≤ 3 |r| + |m| + 2`, regardless of the evaluator's output length.
+[PS10, Algorithm 157.2] -/
+theorem length_spEncStr_le (E : List Bool → List Bool) (k m r : List Bool) :
+    (spEncStr E k m r).length ≤ 3 * r.length + m.length + 2 := by
+  rw [spEncStr_eq, length_pairEncode, List.length_append, List.length_take,
+    List.length_zipWith]
+  omega
+
+/-- For evaluators `E₁`, `E₂`, key length `n`, keys `ks`, plaintext `o` and coins `r`,
+`|(nestedSpecial E₁ E₂ n).enc ks o r| ≤ 7 n + 4`. The bound holds for arbitrary
+evaluator output lengths and arbitrary key-list lengths. [PS10, Protocol 162.6]
+
+**Proof sketch.** With no key the ciphertext is the `n`-bit plaintext. With one key,
+the special-encryption bound gives `4 n + 2`. With two keys, apply it first to the
+inner layer and then to the outer layer to obtain `7 n + 4`. Longer key lists
+produce the empty string. -/
+theorem length_nestedSpecial_enc_le (E₁ E₂ : List Bool → List Bool) (n : ℕ)
+    (ks : List (Fin n → Bool)) (o : Fin n → Bool)
+    (r : (Fin n → Bool) × (Fin n → Bool)) :
+    ((nestedSpecial E₁ E₂ n).enc ks o r).length ≤ 7 * n + 4 := by
+  rw [nestedSpecial_enc]
+  match ks with
+  | [] => simp only [List.length_ofFn]; omega
+  | [k] =>
+    change (spEncStr E₁ (List.ofFn k) (List.ofFn o) (List.ofFn r.1)).length ≤ 7 * n + 4
+    have h := length_spEncStr_le E₁ (List.ofFn k) (List.ofFn o) (List.ofFn r.1)
+    simp only [List.length_ofFn] at h
+    omega
+  | [k₁, k₂] =>
+    change (spEncStr E₂ (List.ofFn k₁)
+      (spEncStr E₁ (List.ofFn k₂) (List.ofFn o) (List.ofFn r.2))
+      (List.ofFn r.1)).length ≤ 7 * n + 4
+    have h₁ := length_spEncStr_le E₁ (List.ofFn k₂) (List.ofFn o) (List.ofFn r.2)
+    have h₂ := length_spEncStr_le E₂ (List.ofFn k₁)
+      (spEncStr E₁ (List.ofFn k₂) (List.ofFn o) (List.ofFn r.2)) (List.ofFn r.1)
+    simp only [List.length_ofFn] at h₁ h₂
+    omega
+  | _ :: _ :: _ :: _ => simp only [List.length_nil]; omega
+
+/-- For every list of words `L`, the history encoding and the word-list encoding agree:
+`Strategy.encodeMsgs L = BinArith.encWords L`. -/
+theorem encodeMsgs_eq_encWords (L : List (List Bool)) :
+    Strategy.encodeMsgs L = BinArith.encWords L := by
+  induction L with
+  | nil => rw [Strategy.encodeMsgs_nil, BinArith.encWords_nil]
+  | cons w L ih => rw [Strategy.encodeMsgs_cons, BinArith.encWords_cons, ih]
+
+/-- For every input count `m`, circuit `C`, seed `s`, gate `i` and assignment `a`,
+the nested-special garbled row has length at most `7 n + 4`.
+[PS10, Protocol 162.6]
+`(garbledRow (nestedSpecial E₁ E₂ n) C s i a).length ≤ 7 * n + 4`. -/
+theorem length_garbledRow_le (E₁ E₂ : List Bool → List Bool) (n : ℕ) {m : ℕ}
+    (C : DAGCircuit m)
+    (s : GarbleSeed C (Fin n → Bool) ((Fin n → Bool) × (Fin n → Bool)))
+    (i : Fin C.gates.length) (a : Fin (C.gates[i]).args.length → Bool) :
+    (garbledRow (nestedSpecial E₁ E₂ n) C s i a).length ≤ 7 * n + 4 := by
+  rw [garbledRow_eq]
+  exact length_nestedSpecial_enc_le E₁ E₂ n _ _ _
+
+/-- Gate `i` of a circuit `C` has at most four garbled rows whenever its argument
+list has length at most two: `|garbledTable M C s i| ≤ 4`.
+[PS10, Construction 158.a] -/
+theorem length_garbledTable_le {K W ρ : Type} [Inhabited K] {m : ℕ}
+    (M : MultiEnc K W ρ) (C : DAGCircuit m) (s : GarbleSeed C K ρ)
+    (i : Fin C.gates.length) (hi : (C.gates[i]).args.length ≤ 2) :
+    (garbledTable M C s i).length ≤ 4 := by
+  rw [garbledTable_eq, List.length_map, Finset.length_toList, Finset.card_univ]
+  simpa only [Fintype.card_fun, Fintype.card_fin, Fintype.card_bool] using
+    (Nat.pow_le_pow_right Nat.zero_lt_two hi : 2 ^ (C.gates[i]).args.length ≤ 2 ^ 2)
+
+/-- For evaluators `E₁`, `E₂`, key length `n`, circuit `C`, seed `s` and a gate `i`
+with at most two arguments, `|encodeMsgs (garbledTable (nestedSpecial E₁ E₂ n) C s i)|
+≤ 56 n + 40`. [PS10, Protocol 162.6]
+
+**Proof sketch.** Each row has at most `7 n + 4` bits and the table has at most four
+rows. Reuse the encoded-word-list bound through the history/word-list encoding
+identity; four words of this length use at most `4 (2 (7 n + 4) + 2)` bits. -/
+theorem length_encoded_garbledTable_le (E₁ E₂ : List Bool → List Bool) (n : ℕ)
+    {m : ℕ} (C : DAGCircuit m)
+    (s : GarbleSeed C (Fin n → Bool) ((Fin n → Bool) × (Fin n → Bool)))
+    (i : Fin C.gates.length) (hi : (C.gates[i]).args.length ≤ 2) :
+    (Strategy.encodeMsgs (garbledTable (nestedSpecial E₁ E₂ n) C s i)).length ≤
+      56 * n + 40 := by
+  rw [encodeMsgs_eq_encWords]
+  calc
+    _ ≤ (garbledTable (nestedSpecial E₁ E₂ n) C s i).length * (2 * (7 * n + 4) + 2) :=
+      BinArith.length_encWords_le _ _ fun w hw => by
+        rw [garbledTable_eq] at hw
+        obtain ⟨a, _, rfl⟩ := List.mem_map.mp hw
+        exact length_garbledRow_le E₁ E₂ n C s i _
+    _ ≤ 4 * (2 * (7 * n + 4) + 2) :=
+      Nat.mul_le_mul_right _ (length_garbledTable_le _ C s i hi)
+    _ = 56 * n + 40 := by omega
+
+/-- For evaluators `E₁`, `E₂`, key length `n`, circuit `C` and seed `s`, if each
+gate has at most two arguments, the encoded list of encoded garbled tables has length
+at most `C.gates.length * (112 n + 82)`. [PS10, Protocol 162.6]
+`(Strategy.encodeMsgs (List.ofFn fun i : Fin C.gates.length => Strategy.encodeMsgs (garbledTable
+(nestedSpecial E₁ E₂ n) C s i))).length ≤ C.gates.length * (112 * n + 82)`. -/
+theorem length_encoded_garbledTables_le (E₁ E₂ : List Bool → List Bool) (n : ℕ)
+    {m : ℕ} (C : DAGCircuit m)
+    (s : GarbleSeed C (Fin n → Bool) ((Fin n → Bool) × (Fin n → Bool)))
+    (hC : ∀ i : Fin C.gates.length, (C.gates[i]).args.length ≤ 2) :
+    (Strategy.encodeMsgs (List.ofFn fun i : Fin C.gates.length =>
+      Strategy.encodeMsgs (garbledTable (nestedSpecial E₁ E₂ n) C s i))).length ≤
+      C.gates.length * (112 * n + 82) := by
+  rw [encodeMsgs_eq_encWords]
+  have h := BinArith.length_encWords_le
+    (List.ofFn fun i : Fin C.gates.length =>
+      Strategy.encodeMsgs (garbledTable (nestedSpecial E₁ E₂ n) C s i))
+    (56 * n + 40) fun w hw => by
+      obtain ⟨i, rfl⟩ := List.mem_ofFn.mp hw
+      exact length_encoded_garbledTable_le E₁ E₂ n C s i (hC i)
+  simpa only [List.length_ofFn, show 2 * (56 * n + 40) + 2 = 112 * n + 82 by omega]
+    using h
+
+/-- Encoding a list of `a` keys of `n` bits has length at most `a (2 n + 2)`:
+`|encodeMsgs (ofFn (fun i => ofFn (keys i)))| ≤ a * (2 n + 2)`.
+[PS10, Protocol 162.6] -/
+theorem length_encoded_keys_le {a n : ℕ} (keys : Fin a → Fin n → Bool) :
+    (Strategy.encodeMsgs (List.ofFn fun i => List.ofFn (keys i))).length ≤
+      a * (2 * n + 2) := by
+  rw [encodeMsgs_eq_encWords]
+  have h := BinArith.length_encWords_le
+    (List.ofFn fun i => List.ofFn (keys i)) n fun w hw => by
+      obtain ⟨i, rfl⟩ := List.mem_ofFn.mp hw
+      exact (List.length_ofFn (f := keys i)).le
+  simpa only [List.length_ofFn] using h
+
+/-- Encoding `o` decoding pairs of two `n`-bit keys has length at most `o (6 n + 6)`:
+`|encodeMsgs (ofFn (fun j => ⟨ofFn (keys j false), ofFn (keys j true)⟩))|
+≤ o * (6 n + 6)`. [PS10, Protocol 162.6, output-decoding implementation] -/
+theorem length_encoded_pairs_le {o n : ℕ} (keys : Fin o → Bool → Fin n → Bool) :
+    (Strategy.encodeMsgs (List.ofFn fun j =>
+      pairEncode (List.ofFn (keys j false)) (List.ofFn (keys j true)))).length ≤
+      o * (6 * n + 6) := by
+  rw [encodeMsgs_eq_encWords]
+  have h := BinArith.length_encWords_le
+    (List.ofFn fun j => pairEncode (List.ofFn (keys j false)) (List.ofFn (keys j true)))
+    (3 * n + 2) fun w hw => by
+      obtain ⟨j, rfl⟩ := List.mem_ofFn.mp hw
+      rw [length_pairEncode, List.length_ofFn, List.length_ofFn]
+      omega
+  simpa only [List.length_ofFn, show 2 * (3 * n + 2) + 2 = 6 * n + 6 by omega] using h
+
+/-- If the table word, input-key word and decoding-pair word satisfy the bounds
+`g (112 n + 82)`, `a (2 n + 2)` and `o (6 n + 6)`, their nested pair has length at most
+`2 g (112 n + 82) + 2 a (2 n + 2) + o (6 n + 6) + 4`.
+[PS10, Protocol 162.6]
+`(pairEncode tables (pairEncode keys pairs)).length ≤ 2 * (g * (112 * n + 82)) + 2 * (a * (2 * n +
+2)) + o * (6 * n + 6) + 4`. -/
+theorem length_message_of_parts_le (tables keys pairs : List Bool) (g a o n : ℕ)
+    (ht : tables.length ≤ g * (112 * n + 82))
+    (hk : keys.length ≤ a * (2 * n + 2))
+    (hp : pairs.length ≤ o * (6 * n + 6)) :
+    (pairEncode tables (pairEncode keys pairs)).length ≤
+      2 * (g * (112 * n + 82)) + 2 * (a * (2 * n + 2)) + o * (6 * n + 6) + 4 := by
+  rw [length_pairEncode, length_pairEncode]
+  omega
+
+end TCSlib.Cryptography.SecureComputation.YaoHybridProof

+/-
+Copyright (c) 2026 TCSlib contributors. All rights reserved.
+Released under Apache 2.0 license as described in the file LICENSE.

+import TCSlib.Cryptography.SecureComputation.Yao.HybridProof.SelectedSeed
+
+set_option maxHeartbeats 0
+set_option relaxedAutoImplicit false
+set_option autoImplicit false
+
+/-!
+# Partial inverse coordinates for selected row nonces
+
+The coordinate maps depend only on the fixed circuit, assignment enumeration, and row
+permutation. A padded occurrence places its challenge nonce in the selected row's first
+or second encryption layer. The reverse map reads its occurrence rank. Unused padded
+slots and unchanged nonce coordinates are represented by `none`.
+
+## Main definitions
+
+* `NonceCoordinates.Index`: a gate, actual assignment, and nonce-half coordinate.
+* `NonceCoordinates.pick`, `place`: partial inverse coordinate maps.
+
+## Main results
+
+* The coordinate maps are exact partial inverses.
+* Reading a filled nonce coordinate agrees with the generic coordinate replacement map.
+
+## References
+
+* [PS10] R. Pass, a. shelat, *A Course in Cryptography*, lecture notes, 3rd ed., January
+  2010, Theorem 163.7 (independent coins at every use of one inactive key).
+-/
+
+namespace TCSlib.Cryptography.SecureComputation.YaoHybridProof.NonceCoordinates
+
+open BoolCircuit
+
+/-- A nonce coordinate consists of a gate, its actual argument assignment, and a half bit:
+`false` denotes the first nonce, and `true` the second.
+`Index C = (Σ i : Fin C.gates.length, (Fin C.gates[i.val].args.length → Bool) × Bool)`. -/
+abbrev Index {m : ℕ} (C : DAGCircuit m) :=
+  Σ i : Fin C.gates.length, (Fin C.gates[i.val].args.length → Bool) × Bool
+
+/-- `Index C` is the dependent product of gate indices and assignment/half pairs.
+`Index C = (Σ i : Fin C.gates.length, (Fin C.gates[i.val].args.length → Bool) × Bool)`. -/
+theorem Index_eq {m : ℕ} (C : DAGCircuit m) :
+    Index C = (Σ i : Fin C.gates.length, (Fin C.gates[i.val].args.length → Bool) × Bool) := rfl
+
+/-- Pick the challenge index for a selected nonce coordinate. The selected argument's half
+is first for position zero and second otherwise; out-of-capacity occurrences return `none`.
+`pick C rows perm u q x = (GateOccurrences.selectedPosition C.gates[x.1.val] x.2.1 u).bind (fun p =>
+if x.2.2 = decide (p.val ≠ 0) then let j := SelectedSeed.slotIndex C rows perm u x.1 x.2.1 p; if h :
+j < q + 1 then some ⟨j, h⟩ else none else none)`. -/
+noncomputable def pick {m : ℕ} (C : DAGCircuit m)
+    (rows : (i : Fin C.gates.length) → List (Fin C.gates[i.val].args.length → Bool))
+    (perm : (i : Fin C.gates.length) → Equiv.Perm (Fin C.gates[i.val].args.length → Bool))
+    (u q : ℕ) (x : Index C) : Option (Fin (q + 1)) :=
+  (GateOccurrences.selectedPosition C.gates[x.1.val] x.2.1 u).bind fun p =>
+    if x.2.2 = decide (p.val ≠ 0) then
+      let j := SelectedSeed.slotIndex C rows perm u x.1 x.2.1 p
+      if h : j < q + 1 then some ⟨j, h⟩ else none
+    else none
+
+/-- `pick` selects the occurrence rank exactly for the selected nonce half and within capacity.
+`pick C rows perm u q x = (GateOccurrences.selectedPosition C.gates[x.1.val] x.2.1 u).bind (fun p =>
+if x.2.2 = decide (p.val ≠ 0) then let j := SelectedSeed.slotIndex C rows perm u x.1 x.2.1 p; if h :
+j < q + 1 then some ⟨j, h⟩ else none else none)`. -/
+theorem pick_eq {m : ℕ} (C : DAGCircuit m)
+    (rows : (i : Fin C.gates.length) → List (Fin C.gates[i.val].args.length → Bool))
+    (perm : (i : Fin C.gates.length) → Equiv.Perm (Fin C.gates[i.val].args.length → Bool))
+    (u q : ℕ) (x : Index C) :
+    pick C rows perm u q x =
+      (GateOccurrences.selectedPosition C.gates[x.1.val] x.2.1 u).bind (fun p =>
+        if x.2.2 = decide (p.val ≠ 0) then
+          let j := SelectedSeed.slotIndex C rows perm u x.1 x.2.1 p
+          if h : j < q + 1 then some ⟨j, h⟩ else none
+        else none) := rfl
+
+/-- Place a padded challenge slot at its gate, actual assignment, and selected nonce half.
+Padding without an occurrence has no destination coordinate.
+`place C rows perm u q j = (GateOccurrences.padded q (GateOccurrences.occurrences C rows perm u)
+j).map (fun s => ⟨s.1, (perm s.1 ((rows s.1).getD s.2.1 (fun _ => false)), decide (s.2.2 ≠
+0))⟩)`. -/
+def place {m : ℕ} (C : DAGCircuit m)
+    (rows : (i : Fin C.gates.length) → List (Fin C.gates[i.val].args.length → Bool))
+    (perm : (i : Fin C.gates.length) → Equiv.Perm (Fin C.gates[i.val].args.length → Bool))
+    (u q : ℕ) (j : Fin (q + 1)) : Option (Index C) :=
+  (GateOccurrences.padded q (GateOccurrences.occurrences C rows perm u) j).map fun s =>
+    ⟨s.1, (perm s.1 ((rows s.1).getD s.2.1 (fun _ => false)), decide (s.2.2 ≠ 0))⟩
+
+/-- `place` maps the padded occurrence triple to its actual-assignment nonce coordinate.
+`place C rows perm u q j = (GateOccurrences.padded q (GateOccurrences.occurrences C rows perm u)
+j).map (fun s => ⟨s.1, (perm s.1 ((rows s.1).getD s.2.1 (fun _ => false)), decide (s.2.2 ≠
+0))⟩)`. -/
+theorem place_eq {m : ℕ} (C : DAGCircuit m)
+    (rows : (i : Fin C.gates.length) → List (Fin C.gates[i.val].args.length → Bool))
+    (perm : (i : Fin C.gates.length) → Equiv.Perm (Fin C.gates[i.val].args.length → Bool))
+    (u q : ℕ) (j : Fin (q + 1)) :
+    place C rows perm u q j =
+      (GateOccurrences.padded q (GateOccurrences.occurrences C rows perm u) j).map (fun s =>
+        ⟨s.1, (perm s.1 ((rows s.1).getD s.2.1 (fun _ => false)), decide (s.2.2 ≠ 0))⟩) := rfl
+
+/-- A padded occurrence `(i,r,p)` is placed at actual assignment `perm i (rows[i][r])`
+and nonce half `decide (p≠0)`. -/
+theorem place_of_padded {m : ℕ} (C : DAGCircuit m)
+    (rows : (i : Fin C.gates.length) → List (Fin C.gates[i.val].args.length → Bool))
+    (perm : (i : Fin C.gates.length) → Equiv.Perm (Fin C.gates[i.val].args.length → Bool))
+    (u q : ℕ) (j : Fin (q + 1)) (i : Fin C.gates.length) (r p : ℕ)
+    (h : GateOccurrences.padded q (GateOccurrences.occurrences C rows perm u) j =
+      some (i, r, p)) :
+    place C rows perm u q j =
+      some ⟨i, (perm i ((rows i).getD r (fun _ => false)), decide (p ≠ 0))⟩ := by
+  rw [place_eq, h, Option.map_some]
+
+/-- Every picked challenge slot is placed back at the original nonce coordinate.
+Completeness of the assignment rows ensures the occurrence rank has the prescribed row.
+`place C rows perm u q j = some x`.
+
+**Proof sketch.** A successful pick identifies the selected argument, the matching nonce
+half, and an in-capacity occurrence rank. Reading that padded rank returns the exact gate,
+row and position. The row-index reader recovers the original actual assignment, and the
+picked half is precisely the half recorded by placement. -/
+theorem place_of_pick {m : ℕ} (C : DAGCircuit m)
+    (rows : (i : Fin C.gates.length) → List (Fin C.gates[i.val].args.length → Bool))
+    (perm : (i : Fin C.gates.length) → Equiv.Perm (Fin C.gates[i.val].args.length → Bool))
+    (u q : ℕ) (hargs : ∀ i : Fin C.gates.length, C.gates[i.val].args.Nodup)
+    (hcomplete : ∀ i a, a ∈ rows i) (x : Index C) (j : Fin (q + 1))
+    (h : pick C rows perm u q x = some j) : place C rows perm u q j = some x := by
+  rcases x with ⟨i, a, half⟩
+  rw [pick_eq] at h
+  obtain ⟨p, hp, hrest⟩ := Option.bind_eq_some_iff.mp h
+  change (if half = decide (p.val ≠ 0) then
+    (if hi : SelectedSeed.slotIndex C rows perm u i a p < q + 1 then
+      some ⟨SelectedSeed.slotIndex C rows perm u i a p, hi⟩ else none) else none) = some j
+    at hrest
+  split_ifs at hrest with hhalf hidx
+  · have heq : (⟨SelectedSeed.slotIndex C rows perm u i a p, hidx⟩ : Fin (q + 1)) = j :=
+      Option.some.inj hrest
+    cases heq
+    obtain ⟨hvertex, hbit⟩ :=
+      (GateOccurrences.selectedPosition_eq_some_iff _ _ _ (hargs i) p).mp hp
+    rw [place_eq, SelectedSeed.slotIndex_padded C rows perm u q i a p (hargs i)
+      (hcomplete i) hvertex hbit hidx, Option.map_some,
+      SelectedSeed.rowIndex_actual C rows perm i a (hcomplete i), ← hhalf]
+
+/-- Every placed challenge slot is picked back at the same index. Duplicate-free row
+and occurrence enumerations make both indices exact.
+`pick C rows perm u q x = some j`.
+
+**Proof sketch.** A successful placement supplies an occurrence triple and its valid row
+and argument indices. Its vertex and bit tests force the selected-position search to choose
+that argument. The occurrence lookup inverts its rank, so the pick uses the original padded
+slot and fits the available capacity. -/
+theorem pick_of_place {m : ℕ} (C : DAGCircuit m)
+    (rows : (i : Fin C.gates.length) → List (Fin C.gates[i.val].args.length → Bool))
+    (perm : (i : Fin C.gates.length) → Equiv.Perm (Fin C.gates[i.val].args.length → Bool))
+    (u q : ℕ) (hargs : ∀ i : Fin C.gates.length, C.gates[i.val].args.Nodup)
+    (hrows : ∀ i, (rows i).Nodup) (x : Index C) (j : Fin (q + 1))
+    (h : place C rows perm u q j = some x) : pick C rows perm u q x = some j := by
+  rw [place_eq] at h
+  obtain ⟨t, ht, hx⟩ := Option.map_eq_some_iff.mp h
+  rcases t with ⟨i, row, p⟩
+  cases hx
+  rw [GateOccurrences.padded_eq] at ht
+  have hmem := List.mem_iff_getElem?.mpr ⟨j.val, ht⟩
+  obtain ⟨hr, hp, hvertex, hbit⟩ :=
+    (GateOccurrences.mem_occurrences_iff C rows perm u i row p (hargs i)).mp hmem
+  have hsel := (GateOccurrences.selectedPosition_eq_some_iff _ _ _ (hargs i) ⟨p, hp⟩).mpr
+    ⟨hvertex, hbit⟩
+  have hidx := SelectedSeed.slotIndex_of_lookup C rows perm u i row j.val ⟨p, hp⟩
+    hr (hrows i) ht
+  rw [pick_eq, hsel, Option.bind_some, if_pos rfl]
+  change (if h : SelectedSeed.slotIndex C rows perm u i
+    (perm i ((rows i).getD row (fun _ => false))) ⟨p, hp⟩ < q + 1 then
+      some ⟨_, h⟩ else none) = some j
+  have hbound : SelectedSeed.slotIndex C rows perm u i
+      (perm i ((rows i).getD row (fun _ => false))) ⟨p, hp⟩ < q + 1 := by
+    rw [hidx]
+    exact j.isLt
+  rw [dif_pos hbound]
+  exact congrArg some (Fin.ext hidx)
+
+/-- Pick and place are exact partial inverses, including unmatched padding and unchanged
+nonce coordinates. These are the hypotheses of the generic coordinate-swap law.
+`pick C rows perm u q x = some j ↔ place C rows perm u q j = some x`. -/
+theorem pick_iff_place {m : ℕ} (C : DAGCircuit m)
+    (rows : (i : Fin C.gates.length) → List (Fin C.gates[i.val].args.length → Bool))
+    (perm : (i : Fin C.gates.length) → Equiv.Perm (Fin C.gates[i.val].args.length → Bool))
+    (u q : ℕ) (hargs : ∀ i : Fin C.gates.length, C.gates[i.val].args.Nodup)
+    (hcomplete : ∀ i a, a ∈ rows i) (hrows : ∀ i, (rows i).Nodup)
+    (x : Index C) (j : Fin (q + 1)) :
+    pick C rows perm u q x = some j ↔ place C rows perm u q j = some x :=
+  ⟨place_of_pick C rows perm u q hargs hcomplete x j,
+    pick_of_place C rows perm u q hargs hrows x j⟩
+
+/-- Reading either half of the filled nonce table is exactly the partial coordinate
+replacement prescribed by `pick`, with the old nonce retained for an unmatched coordinate.
+`(if half then (SelectedSeed.nonce C rows s u q r i a).2 else (SelectedSeed.nonce C rows s u q r i
+a).1) = (pick C rows s.2.2 u q ⟨i, (a, half)⟩).elim (if half then (s.2.1 i a).2 else (s.2.1 i a).1)
+r`.
+
+**Proof sketch.** With no selected argument both halves are retained. With a selected
+argument, distinguish its first/second encryption layer and whether its occurrence fits the
+capacity. The matching half reads its challenge slot; the other half remains fixed. -/
+theorem nonce_read {m : ℕ} {K ρ : Type} (C : DAGCircuit m)
+    (rows : (i : Fin C.gates.length) → List (Fin C.gates[i.val].args.length → Bool))
+    (s : GarbleSeed C K (ρ × ρ)) (u q : ℕ) (r : Fin (q + 1) → ρ)
+    (i : Fin C.gates.length) (a : Fin C.gates[i.val].args.length → Bool) (half : Bool) :
+    (if half then (SelectedSeed.nonce C rows s u q r i a).2
+      else (SelectedSeed.nonce C rows s u q r i a).1) =
+        (pick C rows s.2.2 u q ⟨i, (a, half)⟩).elim
+          (if half then (s.2.1 i a).2 else (s.2.1 i a).1) r := by
+  rw [pick_eq, SelectedSeed.nonce_eq]
+  cases hs : GateOccurrences.selectedPosition C.gates[i.val] a u with
+  | none => simp only [Option.elim_none, Option.bind_none]
+  | some p =>
+    simp only [Option.elim_some, Option.bind_some]
+    cases half <;> by_cases hp : p.val = 0 <;>
+      by_cases hj : SelectedSeed.slotIndex C rows s.2.2 u i a p < q + 1 <;>
+      simp only [hp, hj, ne_eq, not_true_eq_false, not_false_eq_true, decide_true,
+        decide_false, ite_true, ite_false, dite_true, dite_false, Option.elim_some,
+        Option.elim_none, Bool.false_eq_true, Bool.true_eq_false]
+
+end TCSlib.Cryptography.SecureComputation.YaoHybridProof.NonceCoordinates

+/-
+Copyright (c) 2026 TCSlib contributors. All rights reserved.
+Released under Apache 2.0 license as described in the file LICENSE.
+-/
+import TCSlib.Cryptography.SecureComputation.Hybrid
+import TCSlib.Cryptography.SecureComputation.Yao.HybridProof.GateHybridMessage
+
+set_option maxHeartbeats 0
+set_option relaxedAutoImplicit false
+set_option autoImplicit false
+
+/-!
+# Bit-transfer answers as active wire labels
+
+The bit-transfer functionality returns one bit to the receiver in every call. Its
+concatenated answer word can therefore be identified from a product-indexed bit reader.
+The relabeled seed turns the selected input-wire labels into the false labels.
+
+## Main definitions
+
+There are no new definitions.
+
+## Main results
+
+* `length_answersB`: one receiver bit per call gives the exact answer-word length.
+* `answersB_eq_active`: the product-indexed key reader identifies the active-key word.
+* `answersA_nil`: the sender receives an empty answer word.
+
+## References
+
+* [PS10] R. Pass, a. shelat, *A Course in Cryptography*, lecture notes, 3rd ed.,
+  January 2010, Protocol 162.6 (one bit transfer for every key bit).
+-/
+
+namespace TCSlib.Cryptography.SecureComputation.YaoHybridProof.OTAnswerWords
+
+open Complexity Turing BoolCircuit
+
+/-- Bob's concatenated answers to bit transfer have exactly one bit per call:
+`(H.answersB n xs ys rA rB).length = H.calls.eval n`. [PS10, Protocol 162.6] -/
+theorem length_answersB (H : HybridProtocol otFunctionality) (n : ℕ)
+    (xs ys rA rB : List Bool) :
+    (H.answersB n xs ys rA rB).length = H.calls.eval n := by
+  rw [HybridProtocol.answersB_eq, List.length_flatten, List.map_ofFn]
+  change (List.ofFn (fun j : Fin (H.calls.eval n) =>
+    (List.ofFn (otFunctionality.f n (H.callA n xs rA j) (H.callB n ys rB j)).2).length)).sum = _
+  simp only [List.length_ofFn, otFunctionality_outB, PolyExpr.eval_const,
+    List.ofFn_const, List.sum_replicate_nat, Nat.mul_one]
+
+/-- Suppose `count * width` bit-transfer calls return the key bits read at `(i,t)`, and
+the chosen wire values agree with Bob's input word. The full receiver answer word is then
+the false-key word under the relabeled seed. No positive-width assumption is required.
+[PS10, Protocol 162.6]
+`H.answersB n xs ys rA rB = List.ofFn (FakeObjects.activeAnswers C (RelabelGarbling.relabelSeed C v
+s) first count)`.
+
+**Proof sketch.** One bit per call gives the exact total length. Every valid flat index
+comes from the finite product of a wire and a key-bit index. At this index, the supplied
+answer reader and the public seed-relabeling reader give the same bit. List extensionality
+then identifies the complete words. -/
+theorem answersB_eq_active {m width : ℕ} {ρ : Type}
+    (H : HybridProtocol otFunctionality) (n : ℕ) (xs ys rA rB : List Bool)
+    (C : DAGCircuit m) (s : GarbleSeed C (Fin width → Bool) ρ) (first count : ℕ)
+    (v : ℕ → Bool) (hCalls : H.calls.eval n = count * width)
+    (hv : ∀ i : Fin count, v (first + i) = ys.getD i false)
+    (hpoint : ∀ (i : Fin count) (t : Fin width),
+      (H.answersB n xs ys rA rB).getD (i * width + t) false =
+        keyAt C s (first + i) (ys.getD i false) t) :
+    H.answersB n xs ys rA rB =
+      List.ofFn (FakeObjects.activeAnswers C (RelabelGarbling.relabelSeed C v s) first count) := by
+  have hlen := (length_answersB H n xs ys rA rB).trans hCalls
+  have hBit (j : Fin (count * width)) :
+      (H.answersB n xs ys rA rB).getD j false =
+        FakeObjects.activeAnswers C (RelabelGarbling.relabelSeed C v s) first count j := by
+    obtain ⟨⟨i, t⟩, rfl⟩ := (finProdFinEquiv (m := count) (n := width)).surjective j
+    rw [GateHybridMessage.activeAnswers_relabel]
+    change _ = keyAt C s (first + (finProdFinEquiv.symm (finProdFinEquiv (i, t))).1)
+      (v (first + (finProdFinEquiv.symm (finProdFinEquiv (i, t))).1))
+      (finProdFinEquiv.symm (finProdFinEquiv (i, t))).2
+    rw [Equiv.symm_apply_apply, hv]
+    have hi : i * width + t = (finProdFinEquiv (i, t) : ℕ) := by
+      rw [finProdFinEquiv_apply_val]
+      ring
+    rw [← hi, hpoint]
+  apply List.ext_getElem
+  · rw [List.length_ofFn]
+    exact hlen
+  · intro i hi hj
+    have h := hBit ⟨i, by omega⟩
+    simpa only [List.getD_eq_getElem _ _ hi, List.getElem_ofFn] using h
+
+/-- The sender receives no bits from any number of ideal bit transfers:
+`H.answersA n xs ys rA rB = []`. [PS10, Protocol 162.6] -/
+theorem answersA_nil (H : HybridProtocol otFunctionality) (n : ℕ)
+    (xs ys rA rB : List Bool) : H.answersA n xs ys rA rB = [] := by
+  rw [HybridProtocol.answersA_eq]
+  apply List.flatten_eq_nil_iff.mpr
+  intro word hw
+  obtain ⟨j, rfl⟩ := List.mem_ofFn.mp hw
+  apply List.eq_nil_of_length_eq_zero
+  simp only [List.length_ofFn, otFunctionality_outA, PolyExpr.eval_const]
+
+end TCSlib.Cryptography.SecureComputation.YaoHybridProof.OTAnswerWords

+import TCSlib.Cryptography.ZeroKnowledge.EncodingMachines
+
+set_option maxHeartbeats 0
+set_option relaxedAutoImplicit false
+set_option autoImplicit false

+/-!
+# Polynomial-time inputs for bit oblivious transfers
+
+The machines here read the generic layout `⟨1^n, ⟨input, ⟨coins, 1^j⟩⟩⟩`. They
+compute the two candidate coin bits and the input's quotient-indexed choice bit used
+by bitwise oblivious transfers in [PS10, Protocol 162.6]. Their polynomial-time
+contracts include arbitrary, malformed layouts and short coin and input words.
+
+## Main definitions
+
+* `otInputA`: the two coin bits at offsets `inA(n) n + j` and `(S(n) + inA(n)) n + j`.
+* `otInputB`: the input bit at offset `j / n`, with the total division convention at zero.
+
+## Main results
+
+* `polyTimeComputable_otInputA`, `polyTimeComputable_otInputB`: both machines are polynomial-time.
+* `otInputA_layout`, `otInputB_layout`: their equations on canonical call-input layouts.
+
+## References
+
+* [PS10] R. Pass, a. shelat, *A Course in Cryptography*, lecture notes, 3rd ed.,
+  January 2010, Protocol 162.6.
+-/
+
+namespace TCSlib.Cryptography.SecureComputation.YaoHybridProof.OTInputs
+
+open Complexity Turing TCSlib.Cryptography EncodingMachines
+
+/-- The two-bit call-input machine reads `n = |fst z|`,
+`r = fst (snd (snd z))`, and `j = |snd (snd (snd z))|`, and returns
+`[r[inA(n) n + j], r[(S(n) + inA(n)) n + j]]`, with missing bits `false`.
+[PS10, Protocol 162.6] -/
+def otInputA (inA sizeBound : PolyExpr) (z : List Bool) : List Bool :=
+  [(pairFstD (pairSndD (pairSndD z))).getD
+      (inA.eval (pairFstD z).length * (pairFstD z).length +
+        (pairSndD (pairSndD (pairSndD z))).length) false,
+    (pairFstD (pairSndD (pairSndD z))).getD
+      ((sizeBound.eval (pairFstD z).length + inA.eval (pairFstD z).length) *
+        (pairFstD z).length + (pairSndD (pairSndD (pairSndD z))).length) false]
+
+/-- `otInputA inA S z` is the two defaulted coin bits at offsets
+`inA(|fst z|) |fst z| + |snd³ z|` and `(S(|fst z|) + inA(|fst z|)) |fst z| + |snd³ z|`.
+`otInputA inA sizeBound z = [(pairFstD (pairSndD (pairSndD z))).getD (inA.eval (pairFstD z).length *
+(pairFstD z).length + (pairSndD (pairSndD (pairSndD z))).length) false, (pairFstD (pairSndD
+(pairSndD z))).getD ((sizeBound.eval (pairFstD z).length + inA.eval (pairFstD z).length) * (pairFstD
+z).length + (pairSndD (pairSndD (pairSndD z))).length) false]`. -/
+theorem otInputA_eq (inA sizeBound : PolyExpr) (z : List Bool) :
+    otInputA inA sizeBound z =
+      [(pairFstD (pairSndD (pairSndD z))).getD
+          (inA.eval (pairFstD z).length * (pairFstD z).length +
+            (pairSndD (pairSndD (pairSndD z))).length) false,
+        (pairFstD (pairSndD (pairSndD z))).getD
+          ((sizeBound.eval (pairFstD z).length + inA.eval (pairFstD z).length) *
+            (pairFstD z).length + (pairSndD (pairSndD (pairSndD z))).length) false] := rfl
+
+/-- `otInputA inA S ⟨1^n, ⟨x, ⟨r, 1^j⟩⟩⟩` returns the coin bits at offsets
+`inA(n) n + j` and `(S(n) + inA(n)) n + j`, defaulting to `false`.
+`otInputA inA sizeBound (pairEncode (List.replicate n true) (pairEncode x (pairEncode r
+(List.replicate j true)))) = [r.getD (inA.eval n * n + j) false, r.getD ((sizeBound.eval n +
+inA.eval n) * n + j) false]`. -/
+theorem otInputA_layout (inA sizeBound : PolyExpr) (n j : ℕ) (x r : List Bool) :
+    otInputA inA sizeBound (pairEncode (List.replicate n true)
+      (pairEncode x (pairEncode r (List.replicate j true)))) =
+      [r.getD (inA.eval n * n + j) false,
+        r.getD ((sizeBound.eval n + inA.eval n) * n + j) false] := by
+  rw [otInputA_eq]
+  simp only [pairFstD_pairEncode, pairSndD_pairEncode, List.length_replicate]
+
+/-- For polynomial expressions `inA` and `S`, the two-bit input machine
+`otInputA inA S` is polynomial-time on every string. [PS10, Protocol 162.6]
+`PolyTimeComputable (otInputA inA sizeBound)`.
+
+**Proof sketch.** Project the parameter, coin word and call index. Compute both offsets
+with the existing unary polynomial-evaluation, addition and multiplication machines.
+Extract one defaulted coin bit at each offset, and concatenate the singleton words. -/
+theorem polyTimeComputable_otInputA (inA sizeBound : PolyExpr) :
+    PolyTimeComputable (otInputA inA sizeBound) := by
+  have hn := unaryLength_poly polyTimeComputable_pairFstD
+  have htail := polyTimeComputable_pairSndD.comp polyTimeComputable_pairSndD
+  have hj := unaryLength_poly (polyTimeComputable_pairSndD.comp htail)
+  have hr := polyTimeComputable_pairFstD.comp htail
+  have hA := unaryPolyOf_poly inA hn
+  have hS := unaryPolyOf_poly sizeBound hn
+  have h₀ := getBit_poly hr (unaryAdd_poly (unaryMul_poly hA hn) hj)
+  have h₁ := getBit_poly hr (unaryAdd_poly (unaryMul_poly (unaryAdd_poly hS hA) hn) hj)
+  exact (h₀.append h₁).congr fun z => rfl
+
+/-- The one-bit choice-input machine reads `n = |fst z|`, `y = fst (snd z)`,
+and `j = |snd (snd (snd z))|`, and returns `[y.getD (j / n) false]`.
+Division is total, so malformed layouts and `n = 0` are included.
+[PS10, Protocol 162.6] -/
+def otInputB (z : List Bool) : List Bool :=
+  [(pairFstD (pairSndD z)).getD
+    ((pairSndD (pairSndD (pairSndD z))).length / (pairFstD z).length) false]
+
+/-- `otInputB z = [fst(snd z).getD (|snd³ z| / |fst z|) false]`. -/
+theorem otInputB_eq (z : List Bool) :
+    otInputB z = [(pairFstD (pairSndD z)).getD
+      ((pairSndD (pairSndD (pairSndD z))).length / (pairFstD z).length) false] := rfl
+
+/-- `otInputB ⟨1^n, ⟨y, ⟨r, 1^j⟩⟩⟩ = [y.getD (j / n) false]`, including
+short `y` and zero `n`. -/
+theorem otInputB_layout (n j : ℕ) (y r : List Bool) :
+    otInputB (pairEncode (List.replicate n true)
+      (pairEncode y (pairEncode r (List.replicate j true)))) =
+      [y.getD (j / n) false] := by
+  rw [otInputB_eq]
+  simp only [pairFstD_pairEncode, pairSndD_pairEncode, List.length_replicate]
+
+/-- The one-bit choice-input machine `otInputB` is polynomial-time on every string,
+including the zero-parameter case. [PS10, Protocol 162.6]
+`PolyTimeComputable otInputB`.
+
+**Proof sketch.** Read the parameter and call-index lengths in unary, reuse the public
+unary division machine and its quotient equation, and extract the defaulted input bit. -/
+theorem polyTimeComputable_otInputB : PolyTimeComputable otInputB := by
+  have hn := unaryLength_poly polyTimeComputable_pairFstD
+  have htail := polyTimeComputable_pairSndD.comp polyTimeComputable_pairSndD
+  have hj := unaryLength_poly (polyTimeComputable_pairSndD.comp htail)
+  have hy := polyTimeComputable_pairFstD.comp polyTimeComputable_pairSndD
+  have hquot : PolyTimeComputable fun z => List.replicate
+      ((pairSndD (pairSndD (pairSndD z))).length / (pairFstD z).length) true :=
+    (divNum_poly hj hn).congr fun z => by
+      simp only [divNum_eq, Function.comp_apply]
+  exact (getBit_poly hy hquot).congr fun z => rfl
+
+end TCSlib.Cryptography.SecureComputation.YaoHybridProof.OTInputs

@@ -0,0 +1,80 @@
+/-
+Copyright (c) 2026 TCSlib contributors. All rights reserved.
+Released under Apache 2.0 license as described in the file LICENSE.
+-/
+import TCSlib.Cryptography.SecureComputation.Yao.HybridProof.TwoMoveViews
+
+set_option maxHeartbeats 0
+set_option relaxedAutoImplicit false
+set_option autoImplicit false
+
+/-!
+# Message bounds for an Alice-first two-move protocol
+
+The generic transcript equation reduces its message-size bound to the first-message and
+reply bounds, on arbitrary private inputs and tapes. The garbling envelope records the
+explicit polynomial supplied by the generic table, input-key and decoding-pair bounds.
+
+## Main definitions
+
+* `aliceMessageBound`: the polynomial envelope for a garbling message.
+
+## Main results
+
+* `aliceMessageBound_eq`: the envelope is `2 S (112 n + 82) + 2 A (2 n + 2) + O (6 n + 6) + 4`.
+* `twoMove_message_length_le`: two move bounds imply a bound on every transcript message.
+
+## References
+
+* [PS10] R. Pass, a. shelat, *A Course in Cryptography*, lecture notes, 3rd ed.,
+  January 2010, Definition 155.1 and Protocol 162.6 (efficiency and message lengths).
+-/
+
+namespace TCSlib.Cryptography.SecureComputation.YaoHybridProof
+
+open Complexity Turing TCSlib.Cryptography
+
+/-- The garbling-message envelope at `n` is
+`2 S(n) (112 n + 82) + 2 A(n) (2 n + 2) + O(n) (6 n + 6) + 4`.
+[PS10, Protocol 162.6]
+`(aliceMessageBound S A O).eval n = 2 * (S.eval n * (112 * n + 82)) + 2 * (A.eval n * (2 * n + 2)) +
+O.eval n * (6 * n + 6) + 4`. -/
+def aliceMessageBound (S A O : PolyExpr) : PolyExpr :=
+  .add (.add (.add
+    (.mul (.const 2) (.mul S (.add (.mul (.const 112) .var) (.const 82))))
+    (.mul (.const 2) (.mul A (.add (.mul (.const 2) .var) (.const 2)))))
+    (.mul O (.add (.mul (.const 6) .var) (.const 6)))) (.const 4)
+
+/-- Evaluating the garbling-message envelope gives its explicit bound:
+`(aliceMessageBound S A O).eval n = 2 S(n) (112 n + 82) + 2 A(n) (2 n + 2) + O(n) (6 n + 6) + 4`.
+[PS10, Protocol 162.6] -/
+theorem aliceMessageBound_eq (S A O : PolyExpr) (n : ℕ) :
+    (aliceMessageBound S A O).eval n =
+      2 * (S.eval n * (112 * n + 82)) + 2 * (A.eval n * (2 * n + 2)) +
+        O.eval n * (6 * n + 6) + 4 := by
+  simp only [aliceMessageBound, PolyExpr.eval_add, PolyExpr.eval_mul,
+    PolyExpr.eval_const, PolyExpr.eval_var]
+
+/-- In an Alice-first protocol with two messages, bounds `BA(|w|)` and `BB(|w|)` on
+the first message and reply imply bound `(BA + BB)(|w|)` on every transcript message,
+for arbitrary private inputs and tapes. [PS10, Definition 155.1; Protocol 162.6]
+`msg.length ≤ (PolyExpr.add BA BB).eval w.length`. -/
+theorem twoMove_message_length_le (π : Protocol)
+    (msgA : List Bool → List Bool → List Bool → List Bool)
+    (msgB : List Bool → List Bool → List Bool → List Bool → List Bool)
+    (hR : π.rounds = .const 2) (hF : π.verifierFirst = false)
+    (hA : ∀ w z r, π.P.next w z r [] = msgA w z r)
+    (hB : ∀ w z r a, π.V.next w z r [a] = msgB w z r a)
+    (BA BB : PolyExpr)
+    (hLA : ∀ w z r, (msgA w z r).length ≤ BA.eval w.length)
+    (hLB : ∀ w z r a, (msgB w z r a).length ≤ BB.eval w.length)
+    (w zA zB : List Bool) (r : π.ExecCoins π.P w) (msg : List Bool)
+    (hmsg : msg ∈ (π.transcript w zA zB r).1 ++ (π.transcript w zA zB r).2) :
+    msg.length ≤ (PolyExpr.add BA BB).eval w.length := by
+  rw [TwoMoveViews.transcript_eq π msgA msgB hR hF hA hB w zA zB r] at hmsg
+  simp only [List.mem_append, List.mem_singleton] at hmsg
+  rcases hmsg with rfl | rfl
+  · exact (hLB _ _ _ _).trans (by rw [PolyExpr.eval_add]; omega)
+  · exact (hLA _ _ _).trans (by rw [PolyExpr.eval_add]; omega)
+
+end TCSlib.Cryptography.SecureComputation.YaoHybridProof

+/-
+Copyright (c) 2026 TCSlib contributors. All rights reserved.
+Released under Apache 2.0 license as described in the file LICENSE.

+import TCSlib.Cryptography.SecureComputation.Garbling
+import TCSlib.Cryptography.Foundations.BitLists
+
+set_option maxHeartbeats 0
+set_option relaxedAutoImplicit false
+set_option autoImplicit false
+
+/-!
+# Seed-by-seed relabeling of a generic garbling
+
+A wire-value map relabels each wire's two keys, XORs argument assignments,
+readdresses row coins and composes the row permutation with that XOR. The
+original table then has an exact expression in the new seed: its output bit
+is the old gate truth table on the XORed assignment, XOR the output-wire value.
+The statements are generic in the multi-key encryption interface and include
+arbitrary gate arities and default keys outside the circuit.
+
+## Main definitions
+
+* `argValues`: the wire-value map restricted to a gate's argument positions.
+* `relabelSeed`: relabel keys, row coins and row permutations by these values.
+
+## Main results
+
+* `keyAt_relabel`, `argKeys_relabel`: exact key-selection equations.
+* `garbledTable_relabel`: the same enumerated table expressed through the relabeled seed.
+* `inputKeys_relabel`, `output_pair_relabel`: input keys and swapped decoding pairs.
+
+## References
+
+* [PS10] R. Pass, a. shelat, *A Course in Cryptography*, lecture notes, 3rd ed.,
+  January 2010, Theorem 163.7 (the garbling hybrid).
+* [LP09] Y. Lindell, B. Pinkas, *A proof of security of Yao's protocol for
+  two-party computation*, Journal of Cryptology 22(2), 2009, pp. 161–188.
+-/
+
+namespace TCSlib.Cryptography.SecureComputation.YaoHybridProof.RelabelGarbling
+
+open BoolCircuit TCSlib.Cryptography
+
+/-- Restrict a wire-value map `v` to the argument positions of gate `i`:
+`argValues C v i t = v (C.gates[i].args[t])`. -/
+def argValues {m : ℕ} (C : DAGCircuit m) (v : ℕ → Bool) (i : Fin C.gates.length) :
+    Fin (C.gates[i]).args.length → Bool :=
+  fun t => v ((C.gates[i]).args[t])
+
+/-- `argValues C v i = (fun t => v (C.gates[i].args[t]))`. -/
+theorem argValues_eq {m : ℕ} (C : DAGCircuit m) (v : ℕ → Bool)
+    (i : Fin C.gates.length) :
+    argValues C v i = fun t => v ((C.gates[i]).args[t]) := rfl
+
+/-- Argument position `t` reads the wire value at `C.gates[i].args[t]`.
+`argValues C v i t = v ((C.gates[i]).args[t])`. -/
+theorem argValues_apply {m : ℕ} (C : DAGCircuit m) (v : ℕ → Bool)
+    (i : Fin C.gates.length) (t : Fin (C.gates[i]).args.length) :
+    argValues C v i t = v ((C.gates[i]).args[t]) := rfl
+
+/-- Relabel a garbling seed by wire values `v`: key choice `b` becomes `b XOR v w`,
+row coins at assignment `a` come from `a XOR argValues`, and the row permutation
+is composed with that same assignment XOR. [PS10, Theorem 163.7; LP09]
+`relabelSeed C v s = ((fun w b => s.1 w (xor b (v w))), (fun i a => s.2.1 i (xorPerm (argValues C v
+i) a)), fun i => (s.2.2 i).trans (xorPerm (argValues C v i)))`. -/
+def relabelSeed {m : ℕ} (C : DAGCircuit m) (v : ℕ → Bool) {K ρ : Type}
+    (s : GarbleSeed C K ρ) : GarbleSeed C K ρ :=
+  ((fun w b => s.1 w (xor b (v w))),
+    (fun i a => s.2.1 i (xorPerm (argValues C v i) a)),
+    fun i => (s.2.2 i).trans (xorPerm (argValues C v i)))
+
+/-- `relabelSeed C v s` is the tuple of choice-relabeled keys, XOR-addressed row
+coins, and the row permutations composed with assignment XOR.
+`relabelSeed C v s = ((fun w b => s.1 w (xor b (v w))), (fun i a => s.2.1 i (xorPerm (argValues C v
+i) a)), fun i => (s.2.2 i).trans (xorPerm (argValues C v i)))`. -/
+theorem relabelSeed_eq {m : ℕ} (C : DAGCircuit m) (v : ℕ → Bool) {K ρ : Type}
+    (s : GarbleSeed C K ρ) :
+    relabelSeed C v s =
+      ((fun w b => s.1 w (xor b (v w))),
+        (fun i a => s.2.1 i (xorPerm (argValues C v i) a)),
+        fun i => (s.2.2 i).trans (xorPerm (argValues C v i))) := rfl
+
+/-- The relabeled key at wire `w` and choice `b` is `s.1 w (b XOR v w)`.
+`(relabelSeed C v s).1 w b = s.1 w (xor b (v w))`. -/
+theorem relabelSeed_key {m : ℕ} (C : DAGCircuit m) (v : ℕ → Bool) {K ρ : Type}
+    (s : GarbleSeed C K ρ) (w : Fin C.size) (b : Bool) :
+    (relabelSeed C v s).1 w b = s.1 w (xor b (v w)) := rfl
+
+/-- The relabeled row coin at `i,a` is the old coin at `i,a XOR argValues`.
+`(relabelSeed C v s).2.1 i a = s.2.1 i (xorPerm (argValues C v i) a)`. -/
+theorem relabelSeed_coin {m : ℕ} (C : DAGCircuit m) (v : ℕ → Bool) {K ρ : Type}
+    (s : GarbleSeed C K ρ) (i : Fin C.gates.length)
+    (a : Fin (C.gates[i]).args.length → Bool) :
+    (relabelSeed C v s).2.1 i a = s.2.1 i (xorPerm (argValues C v i) a) := rfl
+
+/-- The relabeled row permutation maps `a` to `s.2.2 i a XOR argValues`.
+`(relabelSeed C v s).2.2 i a = xorPerm (argValues C v i) (s.2.2 i a)`. -/
+theorem relabelSeed_perm {m : ℕ} (C : DAGCircuit m) (v : ℕ → Bool) {K ρ : Type}
+    (s : GarbleSeed C K ρ) (i : Fin C.gates.length)
+    (a : Fin (C.gates[i]).args.length → Bool) :
+    (relabelSeed C v s).2.2 i a = xorPerm (argValues C v i) (s.2.2 i a) := rfl
+
+/-- For every vertex `w`, including the default region outside `C.size`,
+`keyAt C (relabelSeed C v s) w b = keyAt C s w (b XOR v w)`. -/
+theorem keyAt_relabel {m : ℕ} (C : DAGCircuit m) (v : ℕ → Bool)
+    {K ρ : Type} [Inhabited K] (s : GarbleSeed C K ρ) (w : ℕ) (b : Bool) :
+    keyAt C (relabelSeed C v s) w b = keyAt C s w (xor b (v w)) := by
+  by_cases hw : w < C.size
+  · simp only [keyAt_eq, dif_pos hw, relabelSeed_key]
+  · simp only [keyAt_eq, dif_neg hw]
+
+/-- The original choice `b` is choice `b XOR v w` in the relabeled seed:
+`keyAt C s w b = keyAt C (relabelSeed C v s) w (b XOR v w)`. -/
+theorem keyAt_original {m : ℕ} (C : DAGCircuit m) (v : ℕ → Bool)
+    {K ρ : Type} [Inhabited K] (s : GarbleSeed C K ρ) (w : ℕ) (b : Bool) :
+    keyAt C s w b = keyAt C (relabelSeed C v s) w (xor b (v w)) := by
+  rw [keyAt_relabel, Bool.xor_assoc, Bool.xor_self, Bool.xor_false]
+
+/-- Argument keys under the relabeled seed at assignment `a` are the original
+argument keys at `a XOR argValues C v i`.
+`argKeys C (relabelSeed C v s) i a = argKeys C s i (xorPerm (argValues C v i) a)`. -/
+theorem argKeys_relabel {m : ℕ} (C : DAGCircuit m) (v : ℕ → Bool)
+    {K ρ : Type} [Inhabited K] (s : GarbleSeed C K ρ) (i : Fin C.gates.length)
+    (a : Fin (C.gates[i]).args.length → Bool) :
+    argKeys C (relabelSeed C v s) i a =
+      argKeys C s i (xorPerm (argValues C v i) a) := by
+  rw [argKeys_eq, argKeys_eq]
+  apply congrArg List.ofFn
+  funext t
+  simp only [keyAt_relabel, xorPerm_apply, argValues_apply]
+
+/-- Encrypting under the relabeled seed at `a`, with output bit
+`gateOut (a XOR argValues) XOR v(output)`, is the original row at
+`a XOR argValues`. This is pointwise in every key and row coin.
+[PS10, Theorem 163.7; LP09]
+`M.enc (argKeys C (relabelSeed C v s) i a) (keyAt C (relabelSeed C v s) (m + i) (xor (gateOut
+(C.gates[i]) (xorPerm (argValues C v i) a)) (v (m + i)))) ((relabelSeed C v s).2.1 i a) = garbledRow
+M C s i (xorPerm (argValues C v i) a)`. -/
+theorem row_relabel {m : ℕ} (C : DAGCircuit m) (v : ℕ → Bool)
+    {K W ρ : Type} [Inhabited K] (M : MultiEnc K W ρ) (s : GarbleSeed C K ρ)
+    (i : Fin C.gates.length) (a : Fin (C.gates[i]).args.length → Bool) :
+    M.enc (argKeys C (relabelSeed C v s) i a)
+        (keyAt C (relabelSeed C v s) (m + i)
+          (xor (gateOut (C.gates[i]) (xorPerm (argValues C v i) a)) (v (m + i))))
+        ((relabelSeed C v s).2.1 i a) =
+      garbledRow M C s i (xorPerm (argValues C v i) a) := by
+  rw [argKeys_relabel, keyAt_relabel, relabelSeed_coin, garbledRow_eq,
+    Bool.xor_assoc, Bool.xor_self, Bool.xor_false]
+
+/-- The original garbled table is exactly the same enumerated list generated
+from the relabeled seed, with plaintext bit
+`gateOut (a XOR argValues) XOR v(output)`. No reordering of the enumeration
+or probabilistic argument is needed. [PS10, Theorem 163.7; LP09]
+`garbledTable M C s i = (Finset.univ : Finset (Fin (C.gates[i]).args.length → Bool)).toList.map (fun
+a => let b := (relabelSeed C v s).2.2 i a; M.enc (argKeys C (relabelSeed C v s) i b) (keyAt C
+(relabelSeed C v s) (m + i) (xor (gateOut (C.gates[i]) (xorPerm (argValues C v i) b)) (v (m + i))))
+((relabelSeed C v s).2.1 i b))`.
+
+**Proof sketch.** At each enumerated assignment, the new row permutation XORs
+the old assignment with the argument values. The transformed-row identity
+converts that ciphertext back to the old seed at a second XOR of those values.
+The two XORs cancel pointwise, leaving exactly the original enumerated row. -/
+theorem garbledTable_relabel {m : ℕ} (C : DAGCircuit m) (v : ℕ → Bool)
+    {K W ρ : Type} [Inhabited K] (M : MultiEnc K W ρ) (s : GarbleSeed C K ρ)
+    (i : Fin C.gates.length) :
+    garbledTable M C s i =
+      (Finset.univ : Finset (Fin (C.gates[i]).args.length → Bool)).toList.map
+        (fun a =>
+          let b := (relabelSeed C v s).2.2 i a
+          M.enc (argKeys C (relabelSeed C v s) i b)
+            (keyAt C (relabelSeed C v s) (m + i)
+              (xor (gateOut (C.gates[i]) (xorPerm (argValues C v i) b)) (v (m + i))))
+            ((relabelSeed C v s).2.1 i b)) := by
+  rw [garbledTable_eq]
+  refine List.map_congr_left fun a _ => ?_
+  dsimp only
+  conv_rhs => rw [row_relabel]
+  rw [relabelSeed_perm]
+  apply congrArg (garbledRow M C s i)
+  funext t
+  simp only [xorPerm_apply, Bool.xor_assoc, Bool.xor_self, Bool.xor_false]
+
+/-- The relabeled input-key list on `x` is the original input-key list on
+`fun i => x i XOR v i`, for all input vertices. -/
+theorem inputKeys_relabel {m : ℕ} (C : DAGCircuit m) (v : ℕ → Bool)
+    {K ρ : Type} [Inhabited K] (s : GarbleSeed C K ρ) (x : Fin m → Bool) :
+    inputKeys C (relabelSeed C v s) x = inputKeys C s (fun i => xor (x i) (v i)) := by
+  rw [inputKeys_eq, inputKeys_eq]
+  apply congrArg List.ofFn
+  funext i
+  exact keyAt_relabel C v s i (x i)
+
+/-- Any selected list of original wire keys is the selected list of relabeled
+keys with choice `bits j XOR v (wire j)`. This includes subsets of input wires
+and repeated selected wires.
+`(List.ofFn fun j => keyAt C s (wire j) (bits j)) = (List.ofFn fun j => keyAt C (relabelSeed C v s)
+(wire j) (xor (bits j) (v (wire j))))`. -/
+theorem selectedKeys_original {m k : ℕ} (C : DAGCircuit m) (v : ℕ → Bool)
+    {K ρ : Type} [Inhabited K] (s : GarbleSeed C K ρ) (wire : Fin k → ℕ)
+    (bits : Fin k → Bool) :
+    (List.ofFn fun j => keyAt C s (wire j) (bits j)) =
+      (List.ofFn fun j => keyAt C (relabelSeed C v s) (wire j)
+        (xor (bits j) (v (wire j)))) := by
+  apply congrArg List.ofFn
+  funext j
+  exact keyAt_original C v s (wire j) (bits j)
+
+/-- The original output-key pair at `w` is the relabeled pair with its two
+entries swapped exactly when `v w = true`. This also holds for default keys
+outside `C.size`. -/
+theorem output_pair_relabel {m : ℕ} (C : DAGCircuit m) (v : ℕ → Bool)
+    {K ρ : Type} [Inhabited K] (s : GarbleSeed C K ρ) (w : ℕ) :
+    (keyAt C s w false, keyAt C s w true) =
+      if v w then
+        (keyAt C (relabelSeed C v s) w true, keyAt C (relabelSeed C v s) w false)
+      else (keyAt C (relabelSeed C v s) w false, keyAt C (relabelSeed C v s) w true) := by
+  cases hv : v w <;> simp [hv, keyAt_relabel]
+
+end TCSlib.Cryptography.SecureComputation.YaoHybridProof.RelabelGarbling
