+/-!
+# Reconstruction from a tuple of slice ciphertexts
+
+A row descriptor retains a fixed ciphertext, reads one challenge slot, or wraps one challenge
+slot under a supplied known outer key. These total word operations never receive the selected
+challenge key. Nested public list maps rebuild tables, and fixed advice supplies the other
+fields of the view. The same machine handles malformed descriptors and challenge encodings.
+
+## Main definitions
+
+* `SliceReconstruction.fixedRecord`, `directRecord`, `wrappedRecord`: row advice records.
+* `SliceReconstruction.row`: the total row interpreter.
+* `SliceReconstruction.table`, `tables`: nested table reconstruction.
+
+## Main results
+
+* `SliceReconstruction.row_poly`: polynomial time on every raw word.
+
+## References
+
+* [PS10] R. Pass, a. shelat, *A Course in Cryptography*, lecture notes, 3rd ed., January
+  2010, Theorem 163.7 (embedding all encryptions under one inactive wire key).
+* [LP09] Y. Lindell, B. Pinkas, *A proof of security of Yao's protocol for two-party
+  computation*, Journal of Cryptology 22(2), 2009, pp. 161–188.
+-/
+
+namespace TCSlib.Cryptography.SecureComputation.YaoHybridProof.SliceReconstruction
+
+open Complexity Turing TCSlib.Cryptography
+
+/-- The fixed-row record is `⟨[], c⟩`, retaining ciphertext `c` unchanged.
+`fixedRecord c = pairEncode [] c`. -/
+def fixedRecord (c : List Bool) : List Bool := pairEncode [] c
+
+/-- `fixedRecord c = ⟨[], c⟩`. -/
+theorem fixedRecord_eq (c : List Bool) : fixedRecord c = pairEncode [] c := rfl
+
+/-- The direct-challenge record is `⟨[true], ⟨1^j, []⟩⟩`, selecting challenge slot `j`.
+`directRecord j = pairEncode [true] (pairEncode (List.replicate j true) [])`. -/
+def directRecord (j : ℕ) : List Bool :=
+  pairEncode [true] (pairEncode (List.replicate j true) [])
+
+/-- `directRecord j = ⟨[true], ⟨1^j, []⟩⟩`. -/
+theorem directRecord_eq (j : ℕ) :
+    directRecord j = pairEncode [true] (pairEncode (List.replicate j true) []) := rfl
+
+/-- The wrapped-challenge record is `⟨[false], ⟨1^j, ⟨k,r⟩⟩⟩`, selecting challenge
+slot `j` and encrypting it under the supplied known outer key `k` with nonce `r`.
+`wrappedRecord j k r = pairEncode [false] (pairEncode (List.replicate j true) (pairEncode k r))`. -/
+def wrappedRecord (j : ℕ) (k r : List Bool) : List Bool :=
+  pairEncode [false] (pairEncode (List.replicate j true) (pairEncode k r))
+
+/-- `wrappedRecord j k r = ⟨[false], ⟨1^j, ⟨k,r⟩⟩⟩`. -/
+theorem wrappedRecord_eq (j : ℕ) (k r : List Bool) :
+    wrappedRecord j k r =
+      pairEncode [false] (pairEncode (List.replicate j true) (pairEncode k r)) := rfl
+
+/-- On `⟨challenge, descriptor⟩`, retain a fixed row, read a challenge slot, or wrap that
+slot under its known key and nonce. Empty tags select fixed rows; `[true]` selects a direct
+slot; every other tag selects wrapping. Out-of-range challenge lookups return `[]`.
+[PS10, Theorem 163.7]
+`row E₂ z = let descriptor := pairSndD z; let tag := pairFstD descriptor; let data := pairSndD
+descriptor; let c := (BinArith.decWords (pairFstD z)).getD (pairFstD data).length []; if tag = []
+then data else if tag = [true] then c else spEncStr E₂ (pairFstD (pairSndD data)) c (pairSndD
+(pairSndD data))`. -/
+def row (E₂ : List Bool → List Bool) (z : List Bool) : List Bool :=
+  let descriptor := pairSndD z
+  let tag := pairFstD descriptor
+  let data := pairSndD descriptor
+  let c := (BinArith.decWords (pairFstD z)).getD (pairFstD data).length []
+  if tag = [] then data else if tag = [true] then c
+  else spEncStr E₂ (pairFstD (pairSndD data)) c (pairSndD (pairSndD data))
+
+/-- `row E₂ z` branches on the descriptor tag and uses the decoded challenge slot at the
+unary index stored in its data, with default `[]`.
+`row E₂ z = let descriptor := pairSndD z; let tag := pairFstD descriptor; let data := pairSndD
+descriptor; let c := (BinArith.decWords (pairFstD z)).getD (pairFstD data).length []; if tag = []
+then data else if tag = [true] then c else spEncStr E₂ (pairFstD (pairSndD data)) c (pairSndD
+(pairSndD data))`. -/
+theorem row_eq (E₂ : List Bool → List Bool) (z : List Bool) :
+    row E₂ z =
+      let descriptor := pairSndD z
+      let tag := pairFstD descriptor
+      let data := pairSndD descriptor
+      let c := (BinArith.decWords (pairFstD z)).getD (pairFstD data).length []
+      if tag = [] then data else if tag = [true] then c
+      else spEncStr E₂ (pairFstD (pairSndD data)) c (pairSndD (pairSndD data)) := rfl
+
+/-- The row interpreter is polynomial-time for polynomial-time `E₂`, on every raw input.
+No selected key is supplied to the interpreter. [PS10, Theorem 163.7]
+`PolyTimeComputable (row E₂)`.
+
+**Proof sketch.** Normalize the challenge word list and apply the existing polynomial-time
+indexed lookup. Descriptor projections give the known wrapping key and nonce. Special
+encryption is polynomial-time on these words, and two word-tag tests select the fixed,
+direct, or wrapped result. Normalization makes every lookup valid even on malformed inputs. -/
+theorem row_poly {E₂ : List Bool → List Bool} (hE₂ : PolyTimeComputable E₂) :
+    PolyTimeComputable (row E₂) := by
+  have htag := polyTimeComputable_pairFstD.comp polyTimeComputable_pairSndD
+  have hdata := polyTimeComputable_pairSndD.comp polyTimeComputable_pairSndD
+  have hindex := polyTimeComputable_unary.comp (polyTimeComputable_pairFstD.comp hdata)
+  have hchallenge := polyTimeComputable_reencodeWords.comp polyTimeComputable_pairFstD
+  have hread := BinArith.polyTimeComputable_getW.comp (hindex.pairEncode hchallenge)
+  have hkey := polyTimeComputable_pairFstD.comp (polyTimeComputable_pairSndD.comp hdata)
+  have hnonce := polyTimeComputable_pairSndD.comp (polyTimeComputable_pairSndD.comp hdata)
+  have hwrap := Garbling.spEncStr_poly hE₂ hkey hread hnonce
+  have hdirect := polyTimeComputable_pairEq.comp
+    (htag.pairEncode (polyTimeComputable_const [true]))
+  have h := polyTimeComputable_ite (polyTimeComputable_isNil.comp htag) hdata
+    (polyTimeComputable_ite hdirect hread hwrap)
+  convert h using 1
+  funext z
+  simp only [row_eq, Function.comp_apply, pairFstD_pairEncode, pairSndD_pairEncode,
+    BinArith.getW_pairEncode, List.length_replicate, decide_eq_true_eq]
+
+/-- A fixed record reconstructs its retained ciphertext unchanged for every challenge word.
+`row E₂ (pairEncode challenge (fixedRecord c)) = c`. -/
+theorem row_fixedRecord (E₂ : List Bool → List Bool) (challenge c : List Bool) :
+    row E₂ (pairEncode challenge (fixedRecord c)) = c := by
+  simp only [row_eq, fixedRecord_eq, pairFstD_pairEncode, pairSndD_pairEncode, ite_true]
+
+/-- Reconstruct an encoded row-descriptor list with one retained challenge tuple.
+`table E₂ z = BinArith.mapW (row E₂) z`. -/
+def table (E₂ : List Bool → List Bool) (z : List Bool) : List Bool :=
+  BinArith.mapW (row E₂) z
+
+/-- `table E₂ z = mapW (row E₂) z`. -/
+theorem table_eq (E₂ : List Bool → List Bool) (z : List Bool) :
+    table E₂ z = BinArith.mapW (row E₂) z := rfl
+
+/-- Reconstructing one encoded table is polynomial-time on every raw word.
+`PolyTimeComputable (table E₂)`. -/
+theorem table_poly {E₂ : List Bool → List Bool} (hE₂ : PolyTimeComputable E₂) :
+    PolyTimeComputable (table E₂) := BinArith.polyTimeComputable_mapW (row_poly hE₂)
+
+/-- On `⟨challenge, descriptors⟩`, reconstruct each decoded row descriptor in order.
+`table E₂ (pairEncode challenge descriptors) = BinArith.encWords ((BinArith.decWords
+descriptors).map fun d => row E₂ (pairEncode challenge d))`. -/
+theorem table_pairEncode (E₂ : List Bool → List Bool) (challenge descriptors : List Bool) :
+    table E₂ (pairEncode challenge descriptors) = BinArith.encWords
+      ((BinArith.decWords descriptors).map fun d => row E₂ (pairEncode challenge d)) :=
+  (table_eq _ _).trans (BinArith.mapW_pairEncode _ _ _)
+
+/-- Reconstruct an encoded list of tables from its encoded lists of row descriptors.
+`tables E₂ z = BinArith.mapW (table E₂) z`. -/
+def tables (E₂ : List Bool → List Bool) (z : List Bool) : List Bool :=
+  BinArith.mapW (table E₂) z
+
+/-- `tables E₂ z = mapW (table E₂) z`. -/
+theorem tables_eq (E₂ : List Bool → List Bool) (z : List Bool) :
+    tables E₂ z = BinArith.mapW (table E₂) z := rfl
+
+/-- Reconstructing all encoded tables is polynomial-time on every raw word.
+`PolyTimeComputable (tables E₂)`. -/
+theorem tables_poly {E₂ : List Bool → List Bool} (hE₂ : PolyTimeComputable E₂) :
+    PolyTimeComputable (tables E₂) := BinArith.polyTimeComputable_mapW (table_poly hE₂)
+
+/-- On `⟨challenge, descriptors⟩`, reconstruct each decoded table-descriptor list in order.
+`tables E₂ (pairEncode challenge descriptors) = BinArith.encWords ((BinArith.decWords
+descriptors).map fun d => table E₂ (pairEncode challenge d))`. -/
+theorem tables_pairEncode (E₂ : List Bool → List Bool) (challenge descriptors : List Bool) :
+    tables E₂ (pairEncode challenge descriptors) = BinArith.encWords
+      ((BinArith.decWords descriptors).map fun d => table E₂ (pairEncode challenge d)) :=
+  (tables_eq _ _).trans (BinArith.mapW_pairEncode _ _ _)
+
+/-- A direct record selects exactly the indexed ciphertext from a canonical challenge list,
+using `[]` as the default for an index beyond the list.
+`row E₂ (pairEncode (BinArith.encWords challenge) (directRecord j)) = challenge.getD j []`. -/
+theorem row_directRecord (E₂ : List Bool → List Bool) (challenge : List (List Bool)) (j : ℕ) :
+    row E₂ (pairEncode (BinArith.encWords challenge) (directRecord j)) =
+      challenge.getD j [] := by
+  simp only [row_eq, directRecord_eq, pairFstD_pairEncode, pairSndD_pairEncode,
+    BinArith.decWords_encWords, List.length_replicate, List.cons_ne_nil, ite_false, ite_true]
+
+/-- A wrapped record encrypts the indexed canonical challenge ciphertext under exactly
+the supplied known outer key and nonce.
+`row E₂ (pairEncode (BinArith.encWords challenge) (wrappedRecord j k r)) = spEncStr E₂ k
+(challenge.getD j []) r`. -/
+theorem row_wrappedRecord (E₂ : List Bool → List Bool) (challenge : List (List Bool))
+    (j : ℕ) (k r : List Bool) :
+    row E₂ (pairEncode (BinArith.encWords challenge) (wrappedRecord j k r)) =
+      spEncStr E₂ k (challenge.getD j []) r := by
+  simp only [row_eq, wrappedRecord_eq, pairFstD_pairEncode, pairSndD_pairEncode,
+    BinArith.decWords_encWords, List.length_replicate, List.cons_ne_nil, List.cons.injEq,
+    Bool.false_eq_true, false_and, ite_false]
+
+/-- Fixed reconstruction advice is `⟨tableDescriptors, ⟨messageTail, ⟨privateInput,aliceOut⟩⟩⟩`.
+Its fields contain no selected challenge key or challenge nonces.
+`advice d t x a = pairEncode d (pairEncode t (pairEncode x a))`. -/
+def advice (tableDescriptors messageTail privateInput aliceOut : List Bool) : List Bool :=
+  pairEncode tableDescriptors (pairEncode messageTail (pairEncode privateInput aliceOut))
+
+/-- `advice d t x a = ⟨d, ⟨t, ⟨x,a⟩⟩⟩`. -/
+theorem advice_eq (d t x a : List Bool) :
+    advice d t x a = pairEncode d (pairEncode t (pairEncode x a)) := rfl
+
+/-- On `⟨N,⟨challenge,advice⟩⟩`, reconstruct the tables, append the fixed message tail,
+and encode the view with fixed private input, empty tape, and that sole received message.
+Pair the view with the retained Alice output. [PS10, Theorem 163.7]
+`rebuild E₂ z = let data := pairSndD (pairSndD z); let rest := pairSndD data; let message :=
+pairEncode (tables E₂ (pairEncode (pairFstD (pairSndD z)) (pairFstD data))) (pairFstD rest);
+pairEncode (⟨pairFstD z, pairFstD (pairSndD rest), [], [message]⟩ : View).encode (pairSndD (pairSndD
+rest))`. -/
+def rebuild (E₂ : List Bool → List Bool) (z : List Bool) : List Bool :=
+  let data := pairSndD (pairSndD z)
+  let rest := pairSndD data
+  let message := pairEncode
+    (tables E₂ (pairEncode (pairFstD (pairSndD z)) (pairFstD data))) (pairFstD rest)
+  pairEncode (⟨pairFstD z, pairFstD (pairSndD rest), [], [message]⟩ : View).encode
+    (pairSndD (pairSndD rest))
+
+/-- `rebuild E₂ z` assembles the reconstructed table word and three fixed advice fields
+into the encoded view and retained Alice output.
+`rebuild E₂ z = let data := pairSndD (pairSndD z); let rest := pairSndD data; let message :=
+pairEncode (tables E₂ (pairEncode (pairFstD (pairSndD z)) (pairFstD data))) (pairFstD rest);
+pairEncode (⟨pairFstD z, pairFstD (pairSndD rest), [], [message]⟩ : View).encode (pairSndD (pairSndD
+rest))`. -/
+theorem rebuild_eq (E₂ : List Bool → List Bool) (z : List Bool) :
+    rebuild E₂ z =
+      let data := pairSndD (pairSndD z)
+      let rest := pairSndD data
+      let message := pairEncode
+        (tables E₂ (pairEncode (pairFstD (pairSndD z)) (pairFstD data))) (pairFstD rest)
+      pairEncode (⟨pairFstD z, pairFstD (pairSndD rest), [], [message]⟩ : View).encode
+        (pairSndD (pairSndD rest)) := rfl
+
+/-- Reconstructing the entire encoded view/output endpoint is polynomial-time on every
+raw input whenever the known-key outer evaluator is polynomial-time.
+[PS10, Theorem 163.7]
+`PolyTimeComputable (rebuild E₂)`.
+
+**Proof sketch.** Project the challenge tuple and table descriptors, then apply the public
+polynomial-time nested table interpreter. Pair its result with the fixed message tail.
+The other advice fields supply the private input and Alice output. Polynomial-time pair
+assembly encodes the view with empty tape and one received message, then retains Alice's
+output as the endpoint's second component. -/
+theorem rebuild_poly {E₂ : List Bool → List Bool} (hE₂ : PolyTimeComputable E₂) :
+    PolyTimeComputable (rebuild E₂) := by
+  have hdata := polyTimeComputable_pairSndD.comp polyTimeComputable_pairSndD
+  have hrest := polyTimeComputable_pairSndD.comp hdata
+  have hchallenge := polyTimeComputable_pairFstD.comp polyTimeComputable_pairSndD
+  have hdescriptors := polyTimeComputable_pairFstD.comp hdata
+  have ht := (tables_poly hE₂).comp (hchallenge.pairEncode hdescriptors)
+  have htail := polyTimeComputable_pairFstD.comp hrest
+  have hprivate := polyTimeComputable_pairFstD.comp
+    (polyTimeComputable_pairSndD.comp hrest)
+  have halice := polyTimeComputable_pairSndD.comp (polyTimeComputable_pairSndD.comp hrest)
+  have hmessage := ht.pairEncode htail
+  have hnil := polyTimeComputable_const ([] : List Bool)
+  have hview := (polyTimeComputable_pairFstD.pairEncode hprivate).pairEncode
+    (hnil.pairEncode (hmessage.pairEncode hnil))
+  refine (hview.pairEncode halice).congr (g := rebuild E₂) fun z => ?_
+  simp only [rebuild_eq, View.encode_mk, Function.comp_apply,
+    Strategy.encodeMsgs_cons, Strategy.encodeMsgs_nil]
+
+/-- The private-key ciphertext-list encoding is the binary-arithmetic word-list encoding.
+This bridge reuses the existing public concatenation characterization.
+`PrivateKeyScheme.encodeList cs = BinArith.encWords cs`. -/
+theorem encodeList_eq_encWords (cs : List (List Bool)) :
+    PrivateKeyScheme.encodeList cs = BinArith.encWords cs :=
+  (TCSlib.Cryptography.encodeList_eq_flatMap cs).trans (BinArith.encWords_def cs).symm
+
+/-- For any challenge word and canonical nested descriptor lists, reconstruct each row
+in its original table, preserving the order of both tables and rows.
+`tables E₂ (pairEncode challenge (BinArith.encWords (descriptors.map BinArith.encWords))) =
+BinArith.encWords (descriptors.map fun ds => BinArith.encWords (ds.map fun d => row E₂ (pairEncode
+challenge d)))`. -/
+theorem tables_records (E₂ : List Bool → List Bool) (challenge : List Bool)
+    (descriptors : List (List (List Bool))) :
+    tables E₂ (pairEncode challenge (BinArith.encWords (descriptors.map BinArith.encWords))) =
+      BinArith.encWords (descriptors.map fun ds => BinArith.encWords
+        (ds.map fun d => row E₂ (pairEncode challenge d))) := by
+  simp only [tables_pairEncode, table_pairEncode, BinArith.decWords_encWords, List.map_map]
+  apply congrArg BinArith.encWords
+  apply List.map_congr_left
+  intro ds _
+  simp only [Function.comp_apply, BinArith.decWords_encWords]
+
+/-- On canonical challenge and descriptor lists, reconstruction retains table and row order.
+`tables E₂ (pairEncode (BinArith.encWords challenge) (BinArith.encWords (descriptors.map
+BinArith.encWords))) = BinArith.encWords (descriptors.map fun ds => BinArith.encWords (ds.map fun d
+=> row E₂ (pairEncode (BinArith.encWords challenge) d)))`. -/
+theorem tables_encoded (E₂ : List Bool → List Bool) (challenge : List (List Bool))
+    (descriptors : List (List (List Bool))) :
+    tables E₂ (pairEncode (BinArith.encWords challenge)
+      (BinArith.encWords (descriptors.map BinArith.encWords))) =
+        BinArith.encWords (descriptors.map fun ds => BinArith.encWords
+          (ds.map fun d => row E₂ (pairEncode (BinArith.encWords challenge) d))) :=
+  tables_records E₂ (BinArith.encWords challenge) descriptors
+
+/-- The fixed, direct, and wrapped record lengths are respectively `|c|+2`, `2j+6`, and
+`2j+2|k|+|r|+8`. Thus a unary challenge index and the known words give explicit advice costs.
+`(fixedRecord c).length = c.length + 2 ∧ (directRecord j).length = 2 * j + 6 ∧ (wrappedRecord j k
+r).length = 2 * j + 2 * k.length + r.length + 8`. -/
+theorem record_lengths (c : List Bool) (j : ℕ) (k r : List Bool) :
+    (fixedRecord c).length = c.length + 2 ∧
+      (directRecord j).length = 2 * j + 6 ∧
+      (wrappedRecord j k r).length = 2 * j + 2 * k.length + r.length + 8 := by
+  simp only [fixedRecord_eq, directRecord_eq, wrappedRecord_eq, length_pairEncode,
+    List.length_nil, List.length_cons, List.length_replicate]
+  omega
+
+/-- Fixed surrounding advice has length `2|d|+2|t|+2|x|+|a|+6`, where the fields are
+table descriptors, message tail, private input, and retained Alice output.
+`(advice d t x a).length = 2 * d.length + 2 * t.length + 2 * x.length + a.length + 6`. -/
+theorem advice_length (d t x a : List Bool) :
+    (advice d t x a).length = 2 * d.length + 2 * t.length + 2 * x.length + a.length + 6 := by
+  rw [advice_eq]
+  simp only [length_pairEncode]
+  omega
+
+/-- On its canonical layout, reconstruction returns the view with reconstructed tables,
+fixed message tail and private input, paired with the fixed actual Alice output.
+`rebuild E₂ (pairEncode N (pairEncode challenge (advice d t x a))) = pairEncode (⟨N, x, [],
+[pairEncode (tables E₂ (pairEncode challenge d)) t]⟩ : View).encode a`. -/
+theorem rebuild_layout (E₂ : List Bool → List Bool) (N challenge d t x a : List Bool) :
+    rebuild E₂ (pairEncode N (pairEncode challenge (advice d t x a))) =
+      pairEncode
+        (⟨N, x, [], [pairEncode (tables E₂ (pairEncode challenge d)) t]⟩ : View).encode a := by
+  simp only [rebuild_eq, advice_eq, pairFstD_pairEncode, pairSndD_pairEncode]
+
+end TCSlib.Cryptography.SecureComputation.YaoHybridProof.SliceReconstruction

+import TCSlib.Cryptography.SecureComputation.SpecialEncryptionMulti
+import TCSlib.Complexity.Randomized.Closure

+set_option relaxedAutoImplicit false
+set_option autoImplicit false

+/-!
+# Advice and averaging for a slice-encryption reduction
+
+A polynomial-time reconstruction can use polynomial-length advice without depending
+on the challenge key or nonces. The machine below keeps the original distinguisher's
+random tape and runs it at the same security parameter.
+
+## Main definitions
+
+There are no new definitions.
+
+## Main results
+
+* `exists_adviceReduction`: a reconstruction with fixed advice is a non-uniform PPT test.
+* `reconstruction_negligible`: one negligible bound covers all bounded advice choices.
+* `distAdv_fibers_le`: a uniform bound survives averaging over auxiliary randomness.
+* `distAdv_chain_le`: a chain of `t` bounded steps has advantage at most `t ε`.
+* `chain_negligible`: a polynomially bounded chain preserves a uniform negligible bound.
+
+## References
+
+* [PS10] R. Pass, a. shelat, *A Course in Cryptography*, lecture notes, 3rd ed.,
+  January 2010, Theorem 163.7 and the hybrid argument in §6.2.3.
+* [Gar26] S. Garg, *A Course in Theory of Cryptography*, UC Berkeley lecture notes,
+  draft of 22 January 2026, Lemma 3.3 (the hybrid argument).
+-/
+
+namespace TCSlib.Cryptography.SecureComputation.YaoHybridProof.SliceReduction
+
+open Complexity Turing TCSlib.Cryptography
+
+/-- For a polynomial-time `rebuild` and advice `β n` of polynomially bounded length,
+there is one non-uniform PPT test that runs `D` on
+`rebuild ⟨1^n, ⟨challenge, β n⟩⟩`, at parameter `n`, with the same random bits.
+[PS10, Theorem 163.7, non-uniform reduction]
+`∃ R : NUPPT, ∃ hcoins : R.coins = D.coins, ∀ n challenge (r : D.Coins n), R.distinguisherL n
+challenge (NUPPT.coinsCongr hcoins.symm n r) = D.distinguisherL n (rebuild (pairEncode
+(List.replicate n true) (pairEncode challenge (β n)))) r`.
+
+**Proof sketch.** Use the existing parameter-aware composition constructor to run the
+reconstruction before `D`, then the advice constructor to fix `β`. The published coin
+congruence preserves the entire tape, so the resulting tests agree pointwise. -/
+theorem exists_adviceReduction (D : NUPPT) {rebuild : List Bool → List Bool}
+    (hRebuild : PolyTimeComputable rebuild) (β : ℕ → List Bool) (b : PolyExpr)
+    (hβ : ∀ n, (β n).length ≤ b.eval n) :
+    ∃ R : NUPPT, ∃ hcoins : R.coins = D.coins,
+      ∀ n challenge (r : D.Coins n),
+        R.distinguisherL n challenge (NUPPT.coinsCongr hcoins.symm n r) =
+          D.distinguisherL n
+            (rebuild (pairEncode (List.replicate n true) (pairEncode challenge (β n)))) r := by
+  obtain ⟨C, hCc, _, _, hC⟩ :=
+    D.exists_compParam hRebuild polyTimeComputable_pairSndD
+  obtain ⟨R, hRc, hR⟩ := C.exists_withAdvice β b hβ
+  refine ⟨R, hRc.trans hCc, ?_⟩
+  intro n challenge r
+  rw [NUPPT.distinguisherL_eq, NUPPT.distinguisherL_eq, NUPPT.runAt_eq,
+    NUPPT.runAt_eq, NUPPT.coinsCongr_ofFn, hR, hC, pairSndD_pairEncode,
+    NUPPT.advInput_eq]
+
+/-- A polynomial-time reconstruction of two slice-encryption experiments has a negligible
+distinguishing advantage uniformly over a finite family of polynomial-length advice choices.
+Slices and deterministic messages may depend on the choice; the reconstruction is fixed.
+[PS10, Theorem 163.7, fixing worst inputs, hybrid index and auxiliary randomness]
+`∃ ε : ℕ → ℝ, Negligible ε ∧ ∀ n a, distAdv (fun t => rebuild (pairEncode (List.replicate n true)
+(pairEncode (sliceMultiEncDist G n (q.eval n) (offset n a) (len n a) (m₀ n a) t) (advice n a))))
+(fun t => rebuild (pairEncode (List.replicate n true) (pairEncode (sliceMultiEncDist G n (q.eval n)
+(offset n a) (len n a) (m₁ n a) t) (advice n a)))) (D.distinguisherL n) ≤ ε n`.
+
+**Proof sketch.** At each parameter choose the advice option maximizing the reconstructed
+advantage. Hard-wire this polynomial-length option into one reduction, independent of the
+challenge key and nonces. The reduction's pointwise contract and coin equivalence identify
+its two acceptance probabilities with the reconstructed experiments. Slice multi-message
+security gives one negligible bound for this reduction, and maximality extends it to every
+option. This does not assert that a reconstruction or a sampler identity exists. -/
+theorem reconstruction_negligible {L : PolyExpr}
+    {G : ∀ n, (Fin n → Bool) → (Fin n → Bool) → (Fin (L.eval n) → Bool)}
+    (hSecure : SliceMultiMessageSecure G) (D : NUPPT) (q b : PolyExpr)
+    {A : ℕ → Type*} [∀ n, Fintype (A n)] [∀ n, Nonempty (A n)]
+    (advice : ∀ n, A n → List Bool) (hAdvice : ∀ n a, (advice n a).length ≤ b.eval n)
+    {rebuild : List Bool → List Bool} (hRebuild : PolyTimeComputable rebuild)
+    (offset len : ∀ n, A n → Fin (q.eval n + 1) → ℕ)
+    (hFit : ∀ n a i, offset n a i + n + len n a i ≤ L.eval n)
+    (m₀ m₁ : ∀ n a i, Fin (len n a i) → Bool) :
+    ∃ ε : ℕ → ℝ, Negligible ε ∧ ∀ n a,
+      distAdv
+        (fun t => rebuild (pairEncode (List.replicate n true)
+          (pairEncode (sliceMultiEncDist G n (q.eval n) (offset n a) (len n a) (m₀ n a) t)
+            (advice n a))))
+        (fun t => rebuild (pairEncode (List.replicate n true)
+          (pairEncode (sliceMultiEncDist G n (q.eval n) (offset n a) (len n a) (m₁ n a) t)
+            (advice n a)))) (D.distinguisherL n) ≤ ε n := by
+  classical
+  let gap := fun n (a : A n) => distAdv
+    (fun t => rebuild (pairEncode (List.replicate n true)
+      (pairEncode (sliceMultiEncDist G n (q.eval n) (offset n a) (len n a) (m₀ n a) t)
+        (advice n a))))
+    (fun t => rebuild (pairEncode (List.replicate n true)
+      (pairEncode (sliceMultiEncDist G n (q.eval n) (offset n a) (len n a) (m₁ n a) t)
+        (advice n a)))) (D.distinguisherL n)
+  have hMax : ∀ n, ∃ a : A n, ∀ a', gap n a' ≤ gap n a := by
+    intro n
+    obtain ⟨a, _, ha⟩ := Finset.exists_max_image Finset.univ (gap n) Finset.univ_nonempty
+    exact ⟨a, fun a' => ha a' (Finset.mem_univ a')⟩
+  choose μ hμ using hMax
+  obtain ⟨R, hcoins, hRun⟩ := exists_adviceReduction D hRebuild
+    (fun n => advice n (μ n)) b (fun n => hAdvice n (μ n))
+  obtain ⟨ε, hε, hBound⟩ := (sliceMultiMessageSecure_iff G).mp hSecure R q
+  refine ⟨ε, hε, fun n a => (hμ n a).trans ?_⟩
+  have hAccept {σ : Type} [Fintype σ] (X : σ → List Bool) :
+      acceptProb (fun t => rebuild (pairEncode (List.replicate n true)
+        (pairEncode (X t) (advice n (μ n))))) (D.distinguisherL n) =
+          acceptProb X (R.distinguisherL n) := by
+    rw [← EnsembleLemmas.acceptProb_coins_equiv (NUPPT.coinsCongr hcoins.symm n)
+      X (R.distinguisherL n), acceptProb_def, acceptProb_def]
+    congr 1
+    funext t
+    rw [hRun]
+  change distAdv _ _ _ ≤ ε n
+  rw [distAdv_def,
+    hAccept (sliceMultiEncDist G n (q.eval n) (offset n (μ n)) (len n (μ n)) (m₀ n (μ n))),
+    hAccept (sliceMultiEncDist G n (q.eval n) (offset n (μ n)) (len n (μ n)) (m₁ n (μ n)))]
+  simpa only [distAdv_def] using
+    hBound n (offset n (μ n)) (len n (μ n)) (hFit n (μ n)) (m₀ n (μ n)) (m₁ n (μ n))
+
+/-- If every fixed auxiliary seed gives advantage at most nonnegative `ε`, then averaging
+that seed independently of the challenge and distinguisher coins also gives advantage at
+most `ε`. This permits the auxiliary keys, nonces and shifts to be fixed in the reduction.
+[PS10, Theorem 163.7, averaging the remaining randomness]
+`distAdv (fun r : α × β => X r.1 r.2) (fun r : α × β => Y r.1 r.2) D ≤ ε`.
+
+**Proof sketch.** Reassociate the joint sample and test coins, then apply the existing
+product-probability equation. Each acceptance probability becomes the average of its
+fixed-seed probabilities. The public bound for averages of pointwise close functions
+gives the claim. -/
+theorem distAdv_fibers_le {α β γ ρ : Type*} [Fintype α] [Fintype β] [Fintype ρ]
+    (X Y : α → β → γ) (D : γ → ρ → Bool) (ε : ℝ) (hε : 0 ≤ ε)
+    (h : ∀ a, distAdv (X a) (Y a) D ≤ ε) :
+    distAdv (fun r : α × β => X r.1 r.2) (fun r : α × β => Y r.1 r.2) D ≤ ε := by
+  have hAccept (Z : α → β → γ) :
+      acceptProb (fun r : α × β => Z r.1 r.2) D =
+        uexp (fun a => acceptProb (Z a) D) := by
+    simp only [acceptProb_def]
+    rw [← uprob_comp_equiv (Equiv.prodAssoc α β ρ).symm, uprob_prod]
+    rfl
+  rw [distAdv_def, hAccept X, hAccept Y]
+  apply abs_uexp_sub_le hε
+  intro a
+  simpa only [distAdv_def] using h a
+
+/-- If each of `t` consecutive hybrids has distinguishing advantage at most `ε`, then
+the endpoints have advantage at most `t ε`. The assertion includes an empty chain.
+[PS10, Theorem 163.7, the gate hybrid; Gar26, Lemma 3.3]
+`distAdv (H 0) (H t) D ≤ (t : ℝ) * ε`. -/
+theorem distAdv_chain_le {σ γ ρ : Type*} [Fintype σ] [Fintype ρ]
+    (H : ℕ → σ → γ) (D : γ → ρ → Bool) (t : ℕ) (ε : ℝ)
+    (h : ∀ i < t, distAdv (H i) (H (i + 1)) D ≤ ε) :
+    distAdv (H 0) (H t) D ≤ (t : ℝ) * ε := by
+  rw [distAdv_def]
+  refine (abs_sub_le_sum_hybrids (fun i => acceptProb (H i) D) t).trans ?_
+  calc
+    _ ≤ ∑ i ∈ Finset.range t, ε := Finset.sum_le_sum fun i hi => by
+      simpa only [distAdv_def] using h i (Finset.mem_range.mp hi)
+    _ = (t : ℝ) * ε := by
+      simp only [Finset.sum_const, Finset.card_range, nsmul_eq_mul]
+
+/-- A polynomially bounded chain with a negligible bound on every neighboring advantage
+has a negligible endpoint advantage, uniformly over the same input family. Zero steps and
+signed negligible bounds are included. [PS10, Theorem 163.7, summing the gate hybrids]
+`∃ ε : ℕ → ℝ, Negligible ε ∧ ∀ n a, distAdv (H n a 0) (H n a (steps n)) (D.distinguisherL n) ≤ ε n`.
+
+**Proof sketch.** Replace the neighboring bound by its absolute value, which is still
+negligible by the existing domination rule. Apply the finite-chain bound and enlarge the
+number of steps to its polynomial envelope. The public polynomial-times-negligible theorem
+then gives one bound valid for every input. -/
+theorem chain_negligible {I σ : ℕ → Type*} [∀ n, Fintype (σ n)]
+    (H : ∀ n, I n → ℕ → σ n → List Bool) (D : NUPPT) (steps : ℕ → ℕ) (p : PolyExpr)
+    (hSteps : ∀ n, steps n ≤ p.eval n) {μ : ℕ → ℝ} (hμ : Negligible μ)
+    (h : ∀ n a i, i < steps n → distAdv (H n a i) (H n a (i + 1))
+      (D.distinguisherL n) ≤ μ n) :
+    ∃ ε : ℕ → ℝ, Negligible ε ∧ ∀ n a,
+      distAdv (H n a 0) (H n a (steps n)) (D.distinguisherL n) ≤ ε n := by
+  have hAbs : Negligible (fun n => |μ n|) :=
+    hμ.mono ⟨0, fun _ _ => by rw [abs_abs]⟩
+  refine ⟨fun n => (p.eval n : ℝ) * |μ n|,
+    EnsembleLemmas.negligible_polyExpr_mul p hAbs, ?_⟩
+  intro n a
+  refine (distAdv_chain_le (H n a) (D.distinguisherL n) (steps n) |μ n|
+    (fun i hi => (h n a i hi).trans (le_abs_self _))).trans ?_
+  exact mul_le_mul_of_nonneg_right (by exact_mod_cast hSteps n) (abs_nonneg _)
+
+end TCSlib.Cryptography.SecureComputation.YaoHybridProof.SliceReduction

+Released under Apache 2.0 license as described in the file LICENSE.

+import TCSlib.Cryptography.Asymptotic.Sampling
+import TCSlib.Cryptography.Encryption.PrivateKey.FromPRF.HybridLemmas

+set_option maxHeartbeats 0
+set_option relaxedAutoImplicit false
+set_option autoImplicit false

+/-!
+# Statistical switches outside a bad event
+
+Two samplers driven by the same finite coin space have statistical distance at most
+the probability of an event outside which they agree. These contracts specialize the
+published expectation coupling bound to event indicators, for the statistical parts
+of [PS10, Theorem 163.7]. They include empty finite coin spaces.
+
+## Main definitions
+
+This module introduces no definitions.
+
+## Main results
+
+* `statClose_of_eq_off`: agreement outside `Bad` gives closeness at most `Pr[Bad]`.
+* `distAdv_le_of_eq_off`: every randomized distinguisher inherits the same bound.
+
+## References
+
+* [PS10] R. Pass, a. shelat, *A Course in Cryptography*, lecture notes, 3rd ed.,
+  January 2010, Theorem 163.7 (the statistical output switch).
+-/
+
+namespace TCSlib.Cryptography.SecureComputation.YaoHybridProof.StatisticalSwitch
+
+open TCSlib.Cryptography
+
+/-- For samplers `X`, `Y` on the same finite coin space, event `Bad` and bound `δ`,
+`Pr[Bad] ≤ δ` and `X r = Y r` outside `Bad` imply `StatClose X Y δ`.
+[PS10, Theorem 163.7, statistical switch]
+
+**Proof sketch.** For each test set, write both probabilities as expectations of
+its zero-one indicators. The public expectation coupling bound applies because
+the indicators lie in `[0,1]` and agree outside `Bad`; its bound is at most `δ`. -/
+theorem statClose_of_eq_off {σ γ : Type*} [Fintype σ] (X Y : σ → γ)
+    (Bad : σ → Prop) (δ : ℝ) (hBad : uprob Bad ≤ δ)
+    (heq : ∀ r, ¬ Bad r → X r = Y r) : StatClose X Y δ := by
+  classical
+  rw [statClose_iff]
+  intro T
+  rw [uprob_eq_uexp, uprob_eq_uexp]
+  refine (MultiMsg.abs_uexp_sub_le_uprob
+    (fun r => if T (X r) then (1 : ℝ) else 0)
+    (fun r => if T (Y r) then (1 : ℝ) else 0) Bad ?_ ?_ ?_).trans hBad
+  · intro r
+    dsimp only
+    split_ifs <;> norm_num
+  · intro r
+    dsimp only
+    split_ifs <;> norm_num
+  · intro r hr
+    simp only [heq r hr]
+
+/-- For same-coins samplers `X`, `Y` agreeing outside an event of probability at most
+`δ`, every randomized distinguisher `D` has `distAdv X Y D ≤ δ`.
+[PS10, Theorem 163.7, statistical switch] -/
+theorem distAdv_le_of_eq_off {σ γ ρ : Type*} [Fintype σ] [Fintype ρ]
+    (X Y : σ → γ) (Bad : σ → Prop) (δ : ℝ) (hBad : uprob Bad ≤ δ)
+    (heq : ∀ r, ¬ Bad r → X r = Y r) (D : γ → ρ → Bool) :
+    distAdv X Y D ≤ δ :=
+  distAdv_le_of_statClose X Y (statClose_of_eq_off X Y Bad δ hBad heq) D
+
+end TCSlib.Cryptography.SecureComputation.YaoHybridProof.StatisticalSwitch

+import TCSlib.Cryptography.ZeroKnowledge.GraphIsomorphism.Efficiency
+
+set_option maxHeartbeats 0
+set_option relaxedAutoImplicit false
+set_option autoImplicit false

+/-!
+# Efficient first-message and reply strategies
+
+The existing interactive-layout machine constructor applies to arbitrary histories.
+These contracts package its two uses in an Alice-first, two-message protocol.
+
+## Main definitions
+
+There are no new definitions.
+
+## Main results
+
+* `firstThenEcho_efficient`: compute the empty-history message and echo a later message.
+* `reply_efficient`: compute a reply from the first received message.
+
+## References
+
+* [PS10] R. Pass, a. shelat, *A Course in Cryptography*, lecture notes, 3rd ed.,
+  January 2010, Definition 155.1 and Protocol 162.6.
+-/
+
+namespace TCSlib.Cryptography.SecureComputation.YaoHybridProof
+
+open Complexity Turing TCSlib.Cryptography
+
+/-- A strategy that computes `first x z r` on the empty history and echoes the first
+received message on every nonempty history is efficient if its first-message map is
+polynomial-time on the interactive machine layout. [PS10, Definition 155.1]
+`S.Efficient`.
+
+**Proof sketch.** Test whether the encoded history is empty. The two branches are the
+given polynomial-time map and the first projection of the encoded history. Apply the
+existing generic strategy constructor and its canonical-layout readers. -/
+theorem firstThenEcho_efficient (S : Strategy)
+    (first : List Bool → List Bool → List Bool → List Bool)
+    (hNil : ∀ x z r, S.next x z r [] = first x z r)
+    (hCons : ∀ x z r a hist, S.next x z r (a :: hist) = a)
+    (hFirst : PolyTimeComputable fun w => first (GI.argX w) (GI.argZ w) (NUPPT.inCoins w)) :
+    S.Efficient := by
+  have hnil : PolyTimeComputable fun w => [decide (GI.argM w = [])] :=
+    polyTimeComputable_isNil.comp GI.argM_poly
+  have hhead := polyTimeComputable_pairFstD.comp GI.argM_poly
+  refine GI.efficient_of_polyTime S (polyTimeComputable_ite hnil hFirst hhead) ?_
+  intro x z r hist
+  cases hist with
+  | nil =>
+    rw [hNil]
+    simp only [GI.argM_ilayout, Strategy.encodeMsgs_nil, decide_true, if_true,
+      GI.argX_ilayout, GI.argZ_ilayout, GI.inCoins_ilayout]
+  | cons a hist =>
+    rw [hCons]
+    simp only [Function.comp_apply, GI.argM_ilayout, Strategy.encodeMsgs_cons, pairEncode_ne_nil,
+      decide_false, Bool.false_eq_true, if_false, pairFstD_pairEncode]
+
+/-- A strategy whose next message is `reply x z (hist.headD [])` is efficient if
+the corresponding interactive-layout map is polynomial-time. The contract includes
+empty and malformed histories and arbitrary private words. [PS10, Definition 155.1]
+`S.Efficient`. -/
+theorem reply_efficient (S : Strategy)
+    (reply : List Bool → List Bool → List Bool → List Bool)
+    (hNext : ∀ x z r hist, S.next x z r hist = reply x z (hist.headD []))
+    (hReply : PolyTimeComputable fun w =>
+      reply (GI.argX w) (GI.argZ w) (pairFstD (GI.argM w))) : S.Efficient := by
+  refine GI.efficient_of_polyTime S hReply ?_
+  intro x z r hist
+  rw [hNext, GI.argX_ilayout, GI.argZ_ilayout, GI.argM_ilayout,
+    Strategy.pairFstD_encodeMsgs]
+
+end TCSlib.Cryptography.SecureComputation.YaoHybridProof

+import TCSlib.Cryptography.SecureComputation.Yao.HybridProof.RowFromCoins

+set_option maxHeartbeats 0
+set_option relaxedAutoImplicit false
+set_option autoImplicit false

+/-!
+# Polynomial-time garbled tables from coin words
+
+The total table adapter reads an encoded argument list. It computes the table only for
+arity at most two, using three arbitrary constant assignment enumerations; higher arities
+return the empty word. Thus malformed descriptions cannot induce exponential enumeration.
+
+## Main definitions
+
+* `TablesFromCoins.tableWord`: the encoded row list of one raw gate description.
+
+## Main results
+
+* `TablesFromCoins.tableWord_poly`: polynomial time on every string.
+* `TablesFromCoins.tableWord_garbledTable`: identification through a row-level contract.
+
+## References
+
+* [PS10] R. Pass, a. shelat, *A Course in Cryptography*, lecture notes, 3rd ed., January
+  2010, Protocol 162.6 (at most four encrypted rows per gate).
+-/
+
+namespace TCSlib.Cryptography.SecureComputation.YaoHybridProof.TablesFromCoins
+
+open Complexity Turing BoolCircuit TCSlib.Cryptography.EncodingMachines
+
+/-- Encode a supplied row family using the argument-word count capped at two; counts
+larger than two return `[]`. The row family can select either real or simulated plaintexts.
+`tableWordWith args row z = let count := (BinArith.decWords (args z)).length; let arity := min count
+2; if count ≤ 2 then BinArith.encWords ((Finset.univ : Finset (Fin arity → Bool)).toList.map fun a
+=> row arity a z) else []`. -/
+noncomputable def tableWordWith (args : List Bool → List Bool)
+    (row : ∀ l : ℕ, (Fin l → Bool) → List Bool → List Bool) (z : List Bool) : List Bool :=
+  let count := (BinArith.decWords (args z)).length
+  let arity := min count 2
+  if count ≤ 2 then BinArith.encWords
+    ((Finset.univ : Finset (Fin arity → Bool)).toList.map fun a => row arity a z)
+  else []
+
+/-- `tableWordWith args row z` is the encoded fixed-enumeration row list for `count ≤ 2`,
+and is `[]` for larger decoded argument counts. -/
+theorem tableWordWith_eq (args : List Bool → List Bool)
+    (row : ∀ l : ℕ, (Fin l → Bool) → List Bool → List Bool) (z : List Bool) :
+    tableWordWith args row z =
+      let count := (BinArith.decWords (args z)).length
+      let arity := min count 2
+      if count ≤ 2 then BinArith.encWords
+        ((Finset.univ : Finset (Fin arity → Bool)).toList.map fun a => row arity a z)
+      else [] := rfl
+
+/-- The encoded row list of the supplied raw gate data for arity at most two; other
+arities return `[]`. The finite assignment list is a constant for each arity.
+[PS10, Protocol 162.6]
+`tableWord E₁ E₂ width bound inputs gate kind args coins z = let count := (BinArith.decWords (args
+z)).length; let arity := min count 2; if count ≤ 2 then BinArith.encWords ((Finset.univ : Finset
+(Fin arity → Bool)).toList.map fun a => RowFromCoins.rowWord E₁ E₂ width bound inputs gate kind args
+coins arity a z) else []`. -/
+noncomputable def tableWord (E₁ E₂ : List Bool → List Bool)
+    (width bound inputs gate : List Bool → ℕ) (kind args coins : List Bool → List Bool)
+    (z : List Bool) : List Bool :=
+  tableWordWith args (fun l a =>
+    RowFromCoins.rowWord E₁ E₂ width bound inputs gate kind args coins l a) z
+
+/-- `tableWord ... z` is the encoded constant-enumeration row list at `min count 2`
+when the decoded argument count is at most two, and is `[]` otherwise.
+`tableWord E₁ E₂ width bound inputs gate kind args coins z = let count := (BinArith.decWords (args
+z)).length; let arity := min count 2; if count ≤ 2 then BinArith.encWords ((Finset.univ : Finset
+(Fin arity → Bool)).toList.map fun a => RowFromCoins.rowWord E₁ E₂ width bound inputs gate kind args
+coins arity a z) else []`. -/
+theorem tableWord_eq (E₁ E₂ : List Bool → List Bool)
+    (width bound inputs gate : List Bool → ℕ) (kind args coins : List Bool → List Bool)
+    (z : List Bool) :
+    tableWord E₁ E₂ width bound inputs gate kind args coins z =
+      let count := (BinArith.decWords (args z)).length
+      let arity := min count 2
+      if count ≤ 2 then BinArith.encWords
+        ((Finset.univ : Finset (Fin arity → Bool)).toList.map fun a =>
+          RowFromCoins.rowWord E₁ E₂ width bound inputs gate kind args coins arity a z)
+      else [] := rfl
+
+/-- The guarded table assembler is polynomial-time if its argument word and every
+supplied row map for the three possible arities are polynomial-time.
+`PolyTimeComputable (tableWordWith args row)`.
+
+**Proof sketch.** Count argument words in unary and cap the count at two. Assemble each
+of the three constant enumeration lists from its polynomial-time row maps, using the
+bounded-arity table contract. A polynomial-time unary comparison rejects larger raw counts. -/
+theorem tableWordWith_poly {args : List Bool → List Bool}
+    (row : ∀ l : ℕ, (Fin l → Bool) → List Bool → List Bool)
+    (hargs : PolyTimeComputable args)
+    (hrow : ∀ l, l ≤ 2 → ∀ a, PolyTimeComputable (row l a)) :
+    PolyTimeComputable (tableWordWith args row) := by
+  -- Counting uses the public encoded-word-list counter.
+  have hcount : PolyTimeComputable fun z =>
+      List.replicate (BinArith.decWords (args z)).length true := by
+    convert BinArith.polyTimeComputable_countW.comp hargs using 1
+    funext z
+    rw [Function.comp_apply, BinArith.countW_eq]
+  have hcap := unaryMin_poly hcount (unaryConst_poly 2)
+  have htable := Garbling.smallArityTable_poly
+    (fun z => min (BinArith.decWords (args z)).length 2)
+    (fun l => (Finset.univ : Finset (Fin l → Bool)).toList) row
+    (fun _ => Nat.min_le_right _ _)
+    (eqTest_poly hcap (unaryConst_poly 0)) (eqTest_poly hcap (unaryConst_poly 1)) hrow
+  -- The guard covers arbitrary malformed argument-list words.
+  convert polyTimeComputable_ite (leTest_poly hcount (unaryConst_poly 2)) htable
+    (polyTimeComputable_const []) using 1
+  funext z
+  simp only [tableWordWith_eq, decide_eq_true_eq]
+
+/-- The total table adapter is polynomial-time if the evaluators, coin/kind/argument words,
+and unary numeric maps are polynomial-time. [PS10, Protocol 162.6]
+`PolyTimeComputable (tableWord E₁ E₂ width bound inputs gate kind args coins)`.
+
+**Proof sketch.** Count the argument words with the public polynomial-time list counter.
+Cap the enumeration arity at two, so the enumeration is one of three fixed finite lists.
+Every row is computed by the polynomial-time raw row machine. The finite-list assembly
+contract gives the encoded table; a unary length test returns the empty word when the
+uncapped count is larger than two. This covers all malformed input strings. -/
+theorem tableWord_poly {E₁ E₂ : List Bool → List Bool} (hE₁ : PolyTimeComputable E₁)
+    (hE₂ : PolyTimeComputable E₂) {width bound inputs gate : List Bool → ℕ}
+    {kind args coins : List Bool → List Bool}
+    (hwidth : PolyTimeComputable fun z => List.replicate (width z) true)
+    (hbound : PolyTimeComputable fun z => List.replicate (bound z) true)
+    (hinputs : PolyTimeComputable fun z => List.replicate (inputs z) true)
+    (hgate : PolyTimeComputable fun z => List.replicate (gate z) true)
+    (hkind : PolyTimeComputable kind) (hargs : PolyTimeComputable args)
+    (hcoins : PolyTimeComputable coins) :
+    PolyTimeComputable (tableWord E₁ E₂ width bound inputs gate kind args coins) := by
+  exact tableWordWith_poly _ hargs (fun l _ a =>
+    RowFromCoins.rowWord_poly hE₁ hE₂ hwidth hbound hinputs hgate hkind hargs hcoins l a)
+
+/-- On an encoded unary argument list of length at most two, the guarded table adapter
+is precisely the encoded fixed-enumeration row list at that genuine argument length.
+`tableWordWith args row z = BinArith.encWords ((Finset.univ : Finset (Fin vertices.length →
+Bool)).toList.map fun a => row vertices.length a z)`.
+
+**Proof sketch.** Canonical decoding determines the argument-word count. The fan-in bound
+removes the malformed-word guard and makes the capped arity equal the genuine length.
+Normalize that natural arity before opening the dependent finite assignment enumeration. -/
+theorem tableWordWith_of_encoded (args : List Bool → List Bool)
+    (row : ∀ l : ℕ, (Fin l → Bool) → List Bool → List Bool) (z : List Bool)
+    (vertices : List ℕ) (hfan : vertices.length ≤ 2)
+    (hargs : args z = BinArith.encWords (vertices.map fun v => List.replicate v true)) :
+    tableWordWith args row z = BinArith.encWords
+      ((Finset.univ : Finset (Fin vertices.length → Bool)).toList.map fun a =>
+        row vertices.length a z) := by
+  have hcount : (BinArith.decWords (args z)).length = vertices.length := by
+    rw [hargs, BinArith.decWords_encWords, List.length_map]
+  have hcap : min (BinArith.decWords (args z)).length 2 = vertices.length := by
+    rw [hcount, min_eq_left hfan]
+  let emit (l : ℕ) := BinArith.encWords
+    ((Finset.univ : Finset (Fin l → Bool)).toList.map fun a => row l a z)
+  rw [tableWordWith_eq]
+  change (if (BinArith.decWords (args z)).length ≤ 2 then
+    emit (min (BinArith.decWords (args z)).length 2) else []) = emit vertices.length
+  rw [if_pos (by rw [hcount]; exact hfan), hcap]
+
+/-- On a genuine argument-word list, if every raw row equals the corresponding garbled
+row after the seed permutation, the table adapter equals the encoded garbled table.
+[PS10, Protocol 162.6]
+`tableWord E₁ E₂ width bound inputs gate kind args coins z = BinArith.encWords (garbledTable
+(nestedSpecial E₁ E₂ (width z)) C s i)`.
+
+**Proof sketch.** Canonical decoding identifies the argument count, so the cap and guard
+leave the genuine arity unchanged. The fixed enumeration is then exactly the public
+garbled-table enumeration, and the supplied row identity agrees at every enumerated row. -/
+theorem tableWord_garbledTable (E₁ E₂ : List Bool → List Bool)
+    (width bound inputs gate : List Bool → ℕ) (kind args coins : List Bool → List Bool)
+    (z : List Bool) (C : DAGCircuit (inputs z))
+    (s : GarbleSeed C (Fin (width z) → Bool)
+      ((Fin (width z) → Bool) × (Fin (width z) → Bool)))
+    (i : Fin C.gates.length) (hfan : (C.gates[i]).args.length ≤ 2)
+    (hargs : args z = BinArith.encWords ((C.gates[i]).args.map fun v => List.replicate v true))
+    (hrow : ∀ a,
+      RowFromCoins.rowWord E₁ E₂ width bound inputs gate kind args coins
+          (C.gates[i]).args.length a z =
+        garbledRow (nestedSpecial E₁ E₂ (width z)) C s i (s.2.2 i a)) :
+    tableWord E₁ E₂ width bound inputs gate kind args coins z =
+      BinArith.encWords (garbledTable (nestedSpecial E₁ E₂ (width z)) C s i) := by
+  change tableWordWith args (fun l a =>
+    RowFromCoins.rowWord E₁ E₂ width bound inputs gate kind args coins l a) z = _
+  rw [tableWordWith_of_encoded args _ z (C.gates[i]).args hfan hargs, garbledTable_eq]
+  apply congrArg BinArith.encWords
+  apply List.map_congr_left
+  intro a _
+  exact hrow a
+
+
+/-- Two guarded table adapters agree at their inputs when their argument words agree
+and their supplied row maps agree pointwise at every arity and assignment.
+`tableWordWith args row z = tableWordWith args' row' z'`.
+
+**Proof sketch.** Package each dependent assignment enumeration as a function of its
+natural arity. Rewrite the argument-word equality before opening that enumeration, so both
+sides use the same finite domain. Congruence of the encoded list and its map then reduces
+the table equality to the supplied row equality; both rejected branches are empty. -/
+theorem tableWordWith_congr_at (args args' : List Bool → List Bool)
+    (row row' : ∀ l : ℕ, (Fin l → Bool) → List Bool → List Bool) (z z' : List Bool)
+    (hargs : args z = args' z') (hrow : ∀ l a, row l a z = row' l a z') :
+    tableWordWith args row z = tableWordWith args' row' z' := by
+  let left (l : ℕ) := BinArith.encWords
+    ((Finset.univ : Finset (Fin l → Bool)).toList.map fun a => row l a z)
+  let right (l : ℕ) := BinArith.encWords
+    ((Finset.univ : Finset (Fin l → Bool)).toList.map fun a => row' l a z')
+  rw [tableWordWith_eq, tableWordWith_eq]
+  change (if (BinArith.decWords (args z)).length ≤ 2 then
+    left (min (BinArith.decWords (args z)).length 2) else []) =
+      if (BinArith.decWords (args' z')).length ≤ 2 then
+        right (min (BinArith.decWords (args' z')).length 2) else []
+  rw [hargs]
+  split_ifs
+  · dsimp only [left, right]
+    apply congrArg BinArith.encWords
+    apply List.map_congr_left
+    intro a _
+    exact hrow _ a
+  · rfl
+
+
+end TCSlib.Cryptography.SecureComputation.YaoHybridProof.TablesFromCoins

+/-
+Copyright (c) 2026 TCSlib contributors. All rights reserved.
+Released under Apache 2.0 license as described in the file LICENSE.
+-/
+import TCSlib.Cryptography.SecureComputation.Yao.HybridProof.CoinRelabel
+import TCSlib.Cryptography.SecureComputation.Yao.HybridProof.CoinSeedRelabel
+
+set_option maxHeartbeats 0
+set_option relaxedAutoImplicit false
+set_option autoImplicit false
+
+/-!
+# Actual key and nonce tables separated from tape parameters
+
+The complete tape is split into the actual key/nonce value tables and an
+unchanged parameter retaining every shift bit and every unused key/row block.
+The derived assignment permutations depend only on those retained shifts.
+There is no claim that XOR shifts are uniform over all assignment permutations.
+
+## Main definitions
+
+* `keyEmbedding`, `rowEmbedding`: the actual coordinate embeddings in padded blocks.
+* `Rest`: all shift bits and unused padded blocks.
+* `equiv`: the complete tape as unchanged parameters and actual value tables.
+
+## Main results
+
+* Exact key, nonce-half and shift readers identify the split with addressed tape bits.
+* The seed permutation is determined by the retained shift parameter alone.
+* `seed_eq`, `seed_on_inverse`: the actual seed is its extracted value tables and shifts.
+
+## References
+
+* [PS10] R. Pass, a. shelat, *A Course in Cryptography*, lecture notes, 3rd ed.,
+  January 2010, Protocol 162.6 and Theorem 163.7 (the gate-by-gate hybrid).
+-/
+
+namespace TCSlib.Cryptography.SecureComputation.YaoHybridProof.TapeValues
+
+open BoolCircuit TCSlib.Cryptography
+
+/-- Split a function into its values on an embedding's image and its complement.
+This only composes the existing subtype split with its image equivalence. -/
+private noncomputable def splitEmbedding {ι κ : Type} (e : ι ↪ κ) (V : Type) :
+    (κ → V) ≃ (ι → V) × ({x : κ // x ∉ Set.range e} → V) :=
+  letI : DecidablePred (fun x => x ∈ Set.range e) := fun _ => Classical.propDecidable _
+  (Equiv.piEquivPiSubtypeProd (fun x => x ∈ Set.range e) (fun _ => V)).trans
+    ((Equiv.arrowCongr (Equiv.ofInjective e e.injective).symm (Equiv.refl V)).prodCongr
+      (Equiv.refl _))
+
+/-- The used component reads `f (e i)`. -/
+private theorem splitEmbedding_used {ι κ : Type} (e : ι ↪ κ) (V : Type)
+    (f : κ → V) (i : ι) : (splitEmbedding e V f).1 i = f (e i) := rfl
+
+/-- The unused component retains `f j` at each coordinate outside the embedding image. -/
+private theorem splitEmbedding_unused {ι κ : Type} (e : ι ↪ κ) (V : Type)
+    (f : κ → V) (j : {x : κ // x ∉ Set.range e}) :
+    (splitEmbedding e V f).2 j = f j := rfl
+
+/-- Embed a real key label `(w,b)` as padded block `(b,castLE w)`.
+`keyEmbedding C S hS (w, b) = (b, Fin.castLE hS w)`. -/
+def keyEmbedding {m : ℕ} (C : DAGCircuit m) (S : ℕ) (hS : C.size ≤ S) :
+    (Fin C.size × Bool) ↪ (Bool × Fin S) where
+  toFun x := (x.2, Fin.castLE hS x.1)
+  inj' _ _ h := Prod.ext
+    (Fin.ext (congrArg (fun z : Bool × Fin S => (z.2 : ℕ)) h)) (congrArg Prod.fst h)
+
+/-- `keyEmbedding C S hS (w,b) = (b,Fin.castLE hS w)`. -/
+theorem keyEmbedding_eq {m : ℕ} (C : DAGCircuit m) (S : ℕ) (hS : C.size ≤ S)
+    (w : Fin C.size) (b : Bool) :
+    keyEmbedding C S hS (w, b) = (b, Fin.castLE hS w) := rfl
+
+/-- The size bound also bounds the gate count. -/
+private theorem gateBound {m : ℕ} (C : DAGCircuit m) (S : ℕ) (hS : C.size ≤ S) :
+    C.gates.length ≤ S := by
+  rw [DAGCircuit.size_eq] at hS
+  omega
+
+/-- Embed a real gate/assignment row as its gate coordinate and binary-fold code.
+Only actual assignments are used, leaving padded and unused row codes separate.
+`rowEmbedding C S hA hS ⟨i, a⟩ = (Fin.castLE hG i, CoinSeedRelabel.assignmentEmbedding
+(C.gates[i.val]).args.length (hA i) a)`. -/
+def rowEmbedding {m : ℕ} (C : DAGCircuit m) (S : ℕ)
+    (hA : ∀ i : Fin C.gates.length, (C.gates[i.val]).args.length ≤ 2)
+    (hS : C.size ≤ S) :
+    (Σ i : Fin C.gates.length, (Fin (C.gates[i.val]).args.length → Bool)) ↪ (Fin S × Fin 4) where
+  toFun x := (Fin.castLE (gateBound C S hS) x.1,
+    CoinSeedRelabel.assignmentEmbedding (C.gates[x.1.val]).args.length (hA x.1) x.2)
+  inj' := by
+    rintro ⟨i, a⟩ ⟨j, b⟩ h
+    have hij : i = j := Fin.ext (congrArg (fun x : Fin S × Fin 4 => (x.1 : ℕ)) h)
+    cases hij
+    have hab : a = b :=
+      (CoinSeedRelabel.assignmentEmbedding (C.gates[i.val]).args.length (hA i)).injective
+        (congrArg Prod.snd h)
+    cases hab
+    rfl
+
+/-- `rowEmbedding ... ⟨i,a⟩ = (castLE i,assignmentEmbedding ... a)` for any supplied
+gate-count bound; the cast proof does not affect the coordinate. -/
+theorem rowEmbedding_eq {m : ℕ} (C : DAGCircuit m) (S : ℕ)
+    (hA : ∀ i : Fin C.gates.length, (C.gates[i.val]).args.length ≤ 2)
+    (hS : C.size ≤ S) (hG : C.gates.length ≤ S) (i : Fin C.gates.length)
+    (a : Fin (C.gates[i.val]).args.length → Bool) :
+    rowEmbedding C S hA hS ⟨i, a⟩ = (Fin.castLE hG i,
+      CoinSeedRelabel.assignmentEmbedding (C.gates[i.val]).args.length (hA i) a) := rfl
+
+/-- Decode a two-width row block into its two width-sized nonce functions. -/
+private def halfEquiv (n : ℕ) :
+    (Fin (2 * n) → Bool) ≃ (Fin n → Bool) × (Fin n → Bool) :=
+  (Equiv.arrowCongr (finCongr (two_mul n)) (Equiv.refl Bool)).trans
+    (Fin.appendEquiv n n).symm
+
+/-- The first nonce half reads the first appended block. -/
+private theorem halfEquiv_first (n : ℕ) (q : Fin (2 * n) → Bool) (t : Fin n) :
+    (halfEquiv n q).1 t = q ((finCongr (two_mul n)).symm (Fin.castAdd n t)) := rfl
+
+/-- The second nonce half reads the second appended block. -/
+private theorem halfEquiv_second (n : ℕ) (q : Fin (2 * n) → Bool) (t : Fin n) :
+    (halfEquiv n q).2 t = q ((finCongr (two_mul n)).symm (Fin.natAdd n t)) := rfl
+
+/-- Separate real key blocks from all unused padded key coordinates. -/
+private noncomputable def keyEquiv {m : ℕ} (C : DAGCircuit m) (S n : ℕ)
+    (hS : C.size ≤ S) :
+    (Bool → Fin S → Fin n → Bool) ≃
+      (Fin C.size → Bool → Fin n → Bool) ×
+        ({x : Bool × Fin S // x ∉ Set.range (keyEmbedding C S hS)} → Fin n → Bool) :=
+  (Equiv.curry Bool (Fin S) (Fin n → Bool)).symm.trans
+    ((splitEmbedding (keyEmbedding C S hS) (Fin n → Bool)).trans
+      ((Equiv.curry (Fin C.size) Bool (Fin n → Bool)).prodCongr (Equiv.refl _)))
+
+/-- The used key table reads its original padded key block. -/
+private theorem keyEquiv_used {m : ℕ} (C : DAGCircuit m) (S n : ℕ) (hS : C.size ≤ S)
+    (q : Bool → Fin S → Fin n → Bool) (w : Fin C.size) (b : Bool) :
+    (keyEquiv C S n hS q).1 w b = q b (Fin.castLE hS w) := rfl
+
+/-- The unused key table retains each complementary padded key block. -/
+private theorem keyEquiv_unused {m : ℕ} (C : DAGCircuit m) (S n : ℕ) (hS : C.size ≤ S)
+    (q : Bool → Fin S → Fin n → Bool)
+    (x : {x : Bool × Fin S // x ∉ Set.range (keyEmbedding C S hS)}) :
+    (keyEquiv C S n hS q).2 x = q x.1.1 x.1.2 := rfl
+
+/-- Separate actual assignment rows, decoded into nonce pairs, from every unused row block. -/
+private noncomputable def rowEquiv {m : ℕ} (C : DAGCircuit m) (S n : ℕ)
+    (hA : ∀ i : Fin C.gates.length, (C.gates[i.val]).args.length ≤ 2) (hS : C.size ≤ S) :
+    (Fin S → Fin 4 → Fin (2 * n) → Bool) ≃
+      ((i : Fin C.gates.length) → (Fin (C.gates[i.val]).args.length → Bool) →
+        (Fin n → Bool) × (Fin n → Bool)) ×
+      ({x : Fin S × Fin 4 // x ∉ Set.range (rowEmbedding C S hA hS)} → Fin (2 * n) → Bool) :=
+  (Equiv.curry (Fin S) (Fin 4) (Fin (2 * n) → Bool)).symm.trans
+    ((splitEmbedding (rowEmbedding C S hA hS) (Fin (2 * n) → Bool)).trans
+      (((Equiv.piCurry (fun (i : Fin C.gates.length)
+          (_ : Fin (C.gates[i.val]).args.length → Bool) => Fin (2 * n) → Bool)).trans
+        (Equiv.piCongrRight fun i => Equiv.piCongrRight fun
+          _ : Fin (C.gates[i.val]).args.length → Bool => halfEquiv n)).prodCongr
+        (Equiv.refl _)))
+
+/-- The actual nonce pair is the two halves of its padded gate/assignment block. -/
+private theorem rowEquiv_used {m : ℕ} (C : DAGCircuit m) (S n : ℕ)
+    (hA : ∀ i : Fin C.gates.length, (C.gates[i.val]).args.length ≤ 2) (hS : C.size ≤ S)
+    (q : Fin S → Fin 4 → Fin (2 * n) → Bool) (i : Fin C.gates.length)
+    (a : Fin (C.gates[i.val]).args.length → Bool) :
+    (rowEquiv C S n hA hS q).1 i a =
+      halfEquiv n (q (Fin.castLE (gateBound C S hS) i)
+        (CoinSeedRelabel.assignmentEmbedding (C.gates[i.val]).args.length (hA i) a)) := rfl
+
+/-- The unused row table retains the complete complementary padded row block. -/
+private theorem rowEquiv_unused {m : ℕ} (C : DAGCircuit m) (S n : ℕ)
+    (hA : ∀ i : Fin C.gates.length, (C.gates[i.val]).args.length ≤ 2) (hS : C.size ≤ S)
+    (q : Fin S → Fin 4 → Fin (2 * n) → Bool)
+    (x : {x : Fin S × Fin 4 // x ∉ Set.range (rowEmbedding C S hA hS)}) :
+    (rowEquiv C S n hA hS q).2 x = q x.1.1 x.1.2 := rfl
+
+/-- The retained parameter is every shift bit, every unused key block and every
+unused row block, in that order. Actual key/nonce values are kept separately.
+`Rest C S n hA hS = ((Fin S → Fin 2 → Bool) × ({x : Bool × Fin S // x ∉ Set.range (keyEmbedding C S
+hS)} → Fin n → Bool) × ({x : Fin S × Fin 4 // x ∉ Set.range (rowEmbedding C S hA hS)} → Fin (2 * n)
+→ Bool))`. -/
+abbrev Rest {m : ℕ} (C : DAGCircuit m) (S n : ℕ)
+    (hA : ∀ i : Fin C.gates.length, (C.gates[i.val]).args.length ≤ 2) (hS : C.size ≤ S) :=
+  (Fin S → Fin 2 → Bool) ×
+    ({x : Bool × Fin S // x ∉ Set.range (keyEmbedding C S hS)} → Fin n → Bool) ×
+    ({x : Fin S × Fin 4 // x ∉ Set.range (rowEmbedding C S hA hS)} → Fin (2 * n) → Bool)
+
+/-- `Rest` is the product of the full shift table and the complementary padded
+key and row block functions.
+`Rest C S n hA hS = ((Fin S → Fin 2 → Bool) × ({x : Bool × Fin S // x ∉ Set.range (keyEmbedding C S
+hS)} → Fin n → Bool) × ({x : Fin S × Fin 4 // x ∉ Set.range (rowEmbedding C S hA hS)} → Fin (2 * n)
+→ Bool))`. -/
+theorem Rest_eq {m : ℕ} (C : DAGCircuit m) (S n : ℕ)
+    (hA : ∀ i : Fin C.gates.length, (C.gates[i.val]).args.length ≤ 2) (hS : C.size ≤ S) :
+    Rest C S n hA hS = ((Fin S → Fin 2 → Bool) ×
+      ({x : Bool × Fin S // x ∉ Set.range (keyEmbedding C S hS)} → Fin n → Bool) ×
+      ({x : Fin S × Fin 4 // x ∉ Set.range (rowEmbedding C S hA hS)} →
+        Fin (2 * n) → Bool)) := rfl
+
+/-- The complete tape is in bijection with the retained parameter and the actual
+key/nonce tables. All unused padded coordinates remain in `Rest`.
+This is a composition of the existing block, image-split and product equivalences.
+[PS10, Theorem 163.7]
+`equiv C S n hA hS r = let q := CoinRelabel.blocksEquiv S n r; ((q.2.2, (fun x => q.1 x.1.1 x.1.2),
+(fun x => q.2.1 x.1.1 x.1.2)), (fun w b => q.1 b (Fin.castLE hS w)), (fun i a => ((fun t => q.2.1
+(Fin.castLE hG i) (CoinSeedRelabel.assignmentEmbedding (C.gates[i.val]).args.length (hA i) a)
+((finCongr (two_mul n)).symm (Fin.castAdd n t))), (fun t => q.2.1 (Fin.castLE hG i)
+(CoinSeedRelabel.assignmentEmbedding (C.gates[i.val]).args.length (hA i) a) ((finCongr (two_mul
+n)).symm (Fin.natAdd n t))))))`. -/
+noncomputable def equiv {m : ℕ} (C : DAGCircuit m) (S n : ℕ)
+    (hA : ∀ i : Fin C.gates.length, (C.gates[i.val]).args.length ≤ 2) (hS : C.size ≤ S) :
+    (Fin (10 * S * n + 2 * S) → Bool) ≃ Rest C S n hA hS ×
+      ((Fin C.size → Bool → Fin n → Bool) ×
+        ((i : Fin C.gates.length) → (Fin (C.gates[i.val]).args.length → Bool) →
+          (Fin n → Bool) × (Fin n → Bool))) :=
+  let V := Fin n → Bool
+  let KU := Fin C.size → Bool → V
+  let KR := {x : Bool × Fin S // x ∉ Set.range (keyEmbedding C S hS)} → V
+  let RU := (i : Fin C.gates.length) → (Fin (C.gates[i.val]).args.length → Bool) → V × V
+  let RR := {x : Fin S × Fin 4 // x ∉ Set.range (rowEmbedding C S hA hS)} → Fin (2 * n) → Bool
+  let Shift := Fin S → Fin 2 → Bool
+  let reorder : (KU × KR) × (RU × RR) × Shift ≃ (Shift × KR × RR) × KU × RU :=
+    (Equiv.prodAssoc (KU × KR) (RU × RR) Shift).symm.trans
+      (((Equiv.prodProdProdComm KU KR RU RR).prodCongr (Equiv.refl Shift)).trans
+        ((Equiv.prodAssoc (KU × RU) (KR × RR) Shift).trans
+          ((Equiv.prodComm (KU × RU) ((KR × RR) × Shift)).trans
+            ((Equiv.prodComm (KR × RR) Shift).prodCongr (Equiv.refl (KU × RU))))))
+  (CoinRelabel.blocksEquiv S n).trans
+    (((keyEquiv C S n hS).prodCongr ((rowEquiv C S n hA hS).prodCongr
+      (Equiv.refl Shift))).trans reorder)
+
+/-- The split retains the shift and complementary block functions, and reads the
+actual key blocks and both nonce halves at their embedded padded coordinates.
+`equiv C S n hA hS r = let q := CoinRelabel.blocksEquiv S n r; ((q.2.2, (fun x => q.1 x.1.1 x.1.2),
+(fun x => q.2.1 x.1.1 x.1.2)), (fun w b => q.1 b (Fin.castLE hS w)), (fun i a => ((fun t => q.2.1
+(Fin.castLE hG i) (CoinSeedRelabel.assignmentEmbedding (C.gates[i.val]).args.length (hA i) a)
+((finCongr (two_mul n)).symm (Fin.castAdd n t))), (fun t => q.2.1 (Fin.castLE hG i)
+(CoinSeedRelabel.assignmentEmbedding (C.gates[i.val]).args.length (hA i) a) ((finCongr (two_mul
+n)).symm (Fin.natAdd n t))))))`. -/
+theorem equiv_eq {m : ℕ} (C : DAGCircuit m) (S n : ℕ)
+    (hA : ∀ i : Fin C.gates.length, (C.gates[i.val]).args.length ≤ 2) (hS : C.size ≤ S)
+    (hG : C.gates.length ≤ S) (r : Fin (10 * S * n + 2 * S) → Bool) :
+    equiv C S n hA hS r =
+      let q := CoinRelabel.blocksEquiv S n r
+      ((q.2.2, (fun x => q.1 x.1.1 x.1.2), (fun x => q.2.1 x.1.1 x.1.2)),
+        (fun w b => q.1 b (Fin.castLE hS w)),
+        (fun i a =>
+          ((fun t => q.2.1 (Fin.castLE hG i)
+            (CoinSeedRelabel.assignmentEmbedding (C.gates[i.val]).args.length (hA i) a)
+            ((finCongr (two_mul n)).symm (Fin.castAdd n t))),
+          (fun t => q.2.1 (Fin.castLE hG i)
+            (CoinSeedRelabel.assignmentEmbedding (C.gates[i.val]).args.length (hA i) a)
+            ((finCongr (two_mul n)).symm (Fin.natAdd n t)))))) := rfl
+
+/-- Every retained shift coordinate reads its exact original tape bit, including
+unused gates and argument positions and the zero-width case.
+`(equiv C S n hA hS r).1.1 i t = (List.ofFn r).getD (10 * S * n + 2 * i + t) false`. -/
+theorem equiv_shift {m : ℕ} (C : DAGCircuit m) (S n : ℕ)
+    (hA : ∀ i : Fin C.gates.length, (C.gates[i.val]).args.length ≤ 2) (hS : C.size ≤ S)
+    (r : Fin (10 * S * n + 2 * S) → Bool) (i : Fin S) (t : Fin 2) :
+    (equiv C S n hA hS r).1.1 i t =
+      (List.ofFn r).getD (10 * S * n + 2 * i + t) false := by
+  rw [equiv_eq C S n hA hS (gateBound C S hS)]
+  exact CoinRelabel.blocksEquiv_shift S n r i t
+
+/-- Each extracted real key bit reads the exact original key tape address.
+`(equiv C S n hA hS r).2.1 w b t = (List.ofFn r).getD ((b.toNat * S + w) * n + t) false`. -/
+theorem equiv_key {m : ℕ} (C : DAGCircuit m) (S n : ℕ)
+    (hA : ∀ i : Fin C.gates.length, (C.gates[i.val]).args.length ≤ 2) (hS : C.size ≤ S)
+    (r : Fin (10 * S * n + 2 * S) → Bool) (w : Fin C.size) (b : Bool) (t : Fin n) :
+    (equiv C S n hA hS r).2.1 w b t =
+      (List.ofFn r).getD ((b.toNat * S + w) * n + t) false := by
+  rw [equiv_eq C S n hA hS (gateBound C S hS)]
+  exact CoinRelabel.blocksEquiv_key S n r b (Fin.castLE hS w) t
+
+/-- The extracted first nonce half reads the original row's first-width tape bits.
+`((equiv C S n hA hS r).2.2 i a).1 t = (List.ofFn r).getD (2 * S * n + (4 * i + (List.ofFn a).foldr
+(fun b k => b.toNat + 2 * k) 0) * (2 * n) + t) false`. -/
+theorem equiv_nonce_first {m : ℕ} (C : DAGCircuit m) (S n : ℕ)
+    (hA : ∀ i : Fin C.gates.length, (C.gates[i.val]).args.length ≤ 2) (hS : C.size ≤ S)
+    (r : Fin (10 * S * n + 2 * S) → Bool) (i : Fin C.gates.length)
+    (a : Fin (C.gates[i.val]).args.length → Bool) (t : Fin n) :
+    ((equiv C S n hA hS r).2.2 i a).1 t =
+      (List.ofFn r).getD (2 * S * n + (4 * i +
+        (List.ofFn a).foldr (fun b k => b.toNat + 2 * k) 0) * (2 * n) + t) false := by
+  rw [equiv_eq C S n hA hS (gateBound C S hS)]
+  have h := CoinRelabel.blocksEquiv_row S n r (Fin.castLE (gateBound C S hS) i)
+    (CoinSeedRelabel.assignmentEmbedding (C.gates[i.val]).args.length (hA i) a)
+    ((finCongr (two_mul n)).symm (Fin.castAdd n t))
+  rw [CoinSeedRelabel.assignmentEmbedding_eq] at h
+  exact h
+
+/-- The extracted second nonce half reads the original row's second-width tape bits.
+`((equiv C S n hA hS r).2.2 i a).2 t = (List.ofFn r).getD (2 * S * n + (4 * i + (List.ofFn a).foldr
+(fun b k => b.toNat + 2 * k) 0) * (2 * n) + n + t) false`. -/
+theorem equiv_nonce_second {m : ℕ} (C : DAGCircuit m) (S n : ℕ)
+    (hA : ∀ i : Fin C.gates.length, (C.gates[i.val]).args.length ≤ 2) (hS : C.size ≤ S)
+    (r : Fin (10 * S * n + 2 * S) → Bool) (i : Fin C.gates.length)
+    (a : Fin (C.gates[i.val]).args.length → Bool) (t : Fin n) :
+    ((equiv C S n hA hS r).2.2 i a).2 t =
+      (List.ofFn r).getD (2 * S * n + (4 * i +
+        (List.ofFn a).foldr (fun b k => b.toNat + 2 * k) 0) * (2 * n) + n + t) false := by
+  rw [equiv_eq C S n hA hS (gateBound C S hS)]
+  have h := CoinRelabel.blocksEquiv_row S n r (Fin.castLE (gateBound C S hS) i)
+    (CoinSeedRelabel.assignmentEmbedding (C.gates[i.val]).args.length (hA i) a)
+    ((finCongr (two_mul n)).symm (Fin.natAdd n t))
+  rw [CoinSeedRelabel.assignmentEmbedding_eq] at h
+  simpa only [Nat.add_assoc] using h
+
+/-- Each unused key block is retained verbatim in the unchanged parameter.
+`(equiv C S n hA hS r).1.2.1 x t = (List.ofFn r).getD ((x.1.1.toNat * S + x.1.2) * n + t) false`. -/
+theorem equiv_unused_key {m : ℕ} (C : DAGCircuit m) (S n : ℕ)
+    (hA : ∀ i : Fin C.gates.length, (C.gates[i.val]).args.length ≤ 2) (hS : C.size ≤ S)
+    (r : Fin (10 * S * n + 2 * S) → Bool)
+    (x : {x : Bool × Fin S // x ∉ Set.range (keyEmbedding C S hS)}) (t : Fin n) :
+    (equiv C S n hA hS r).1.2.1 x t =
+      (List.ofFn r).getD ((x.1.1.toNat * S + x.1.2) * n + t) false := by
+  rw [equiv_eq C S n hA hS (gateBound C S hS)]
+  exact CoinRelabel.blocksEquiv_key S n r x.1.1 x.1.2 t
+
+/-- Each unused row block, including unused assignment codes, is retained verbatim.
+`(equiv C S n hA hS r).1.2.2 x t = (List.ofFn r).getD (2 * S * n + (4 * x.1.1 + x.1.2) * (2 * n) +
+t) false`. -/
+theorem equiv_unused_row {m : ℕ} (C : DAGCircuit m) (S n : ℕ)
+    (hA : ∀ i : Fin C.gates.length, (C.gates[i.val]).args.length ≤ 2) (hS : C.size ≤ S)
+    (r : Fin (10 * S * n + 2 * S) → Bool)
+    (x : {x : Fin S × Fin 4 // x ∉ Set.range (rowEmbedding C S hA hS)})
+    (t : Fin (2 * n)) :
+    (equiv C S n hA hS r).1.2.2 x t =
+      (List.ofFn r).getD (2 * S * n + (4 * x.1.1 + x.1.2) * (2 * n) + t) false := by
+  rw [equiv_eq C S n hA hS (gateBound C S hS)]
+  exact CoinRelabel.blocksEquiv_row S n r x.1.1 x.1.2 t
+
+/-- The actual assignment permutation XORs each argument with its retained
+shift bit. It depends only on `Rest`, not on the extracted value table.
+[PS10, Theorem 163.7]
+`permutation C S n hA hS p i = xorPerm (fun t => p.1 (Fin.castLE hG i) (Fin.castLE (hA i) t))`. -/
+def permutation {m : ℕ} (C : DAGCircuit m) (S n : ℕ)
+    (hA : ∀ i : Fin C.gates.length, (C.gates[i.val]).args.length ≤ 2) (hS : C.size ≤ S)
+    (p : Rest C S n hA hS) (i : Fin C.gates.length) :
+    Equiv.Perm (Fin (C.gates[i.val]).args.length → Bool) :=
+  xorPerm (fun t => p.1 (Fin.castLE (gateBound C S hS) i) (Fin.castLE (hA i) t))
+
+/-- `permutation ... p i` is `xorPerm` of the retained argument-shift bits.
+`permutation C S n hA hS p i = xorPerm (fun t => p.1 (Fin.castLE hG i) (Fin.castLE (hA i) t))`. -/
+theorem permutation_eq {m : ℕ} (C : DAGCircuit m) (S n : ℕ)
+    (hA : ∀ i : Fin C.gates.length, (C.gates[i.val]).args.length ≤ 2) (hS : C.size ≤ S)
+    (hG : C.gates.length ≤ S) (p : Rest C S n hA hS) (i : Fin C.gates.length) :
+    permutation C S n hA hS p i =
+      xorPerm (fun t => p.1 (Fin.castLE hG i) (Fin.castLE (hA i) t)) := rfl
+
+/-- At every argument position, the derived permutation XORs the supplied
+assignment bit with exactly that retained shift bit.
+`permutation C S n hA hS p i a t = xor (a t) (p.1 (Fin.castLE hG i) (Fin.castLE (hA i) t))`. -/
+theorem permutation_apply {m : ℕ} (C : DAGCircuit m) (S n : ℕ)
+    (hA : ∀ i : Fin C.gates.length, (C.gates[i.val]).args.length ≤ 2) (hS : C.size ≤ S)
+    (hG : C.gates.length ≤ S) (p : Rest C S n hA hS) (i : Fin C.gates.length)
+    (a : Fin (C.gates[i.val]).args.length → Bool) (t : Fin (C.gates[i.val]).args.length) :
+    permutation C S n hA hS p i a t =
+      xor (a t) (p.1 (Fin.castLE hG i) (Fin.castLE (hA i) t)) := by
+  simp only [permutation_eq C S n hA hS hG, xorPerm_apply]
+
+/-- A seed whose permutation reader XORs the addressed shift bits has exactly
+the permutation derived from the retained tape parameter. No distribution over
+all permutations is asserted. [PS10, Theorem 163.7]
+`(seed (List.ofFn r)).2.2 i a = permutation C S n hA hS (equiv C S n hA hS r).1 i a`.
+
+**Proof sketch.** Compare each assignment position. The seed reader and the
+retained-shift reader give the same bit address; the finite cast reader removes
+the padded index casts. -/
+theorem seed_perm {m : ℕ} {ρ : Type} (C : DAGCircuit m) (S n : ℕ)
+    (hA : ∀ i : Fin C.gates.length, (C.gates[i.val]).args.length ≤ 2) (hS : C.size ≤ S)
+    (seed : List Bool → GarbleSeed C (Fin n → Bool) ρ)
+    (hP : ∀ q (i : Fin C.gates.length) (a : Fin (C.gates[i.val]).args.length → Bool)
+        (t : Fin (C.gates[i.val]).args.length),
+      (seed q).2.2 i a t = xor (a t) (q.getD (10 * S * n + 2 * i + t) false))
+    (r : Fin (10 * S * n + 2 * S) → Bool) (i : Fin C.gates.length)
+    (a : Fin (C.gates[i.val]).args.length → Bool) :
+    (seed (List.ofFn r)).2.2 i a =
+      permutation C S n hA hS (equiv C S n hA hS r).1 i a := by
+  funext t
+  rw [hP, permutation_apply C S n hA hS (gateBound C S hS), equiv_shift]
+  simp only [Fin.coe_castLE]
+
+section Seed
+
+variable {m : ℕ} (C : DAGCircuit m) (S n : ℕ)
+  (hA : ∀ i : Fin C.gates.length, (C.gates[i.val]).args.length ≤ 2) (hS : C.size ≤ S)
+  (seed : List Bool → GarbleSeed C (Fin n → Bool) ((Fin n → Bool) × (Fin n → Bool)))
+  (hK : ∀ q (w : Fin C.size) b (t : Fin n),
+    (seed q).1 w b t = q.getD ((b.toNat * S + w) * n + t) false)
+  (hR₀ : ∀ q (i : Fin C.gates.length) (a : Fin (C.gates[i.val]).args.length → Bool)
+      (t : Fin n),
+    ((seed q).2.1 i a).1 t = q.getD (2 * S * n + (4 * i +
+      (List.ofFn a).foldr (fun b k => b.toNat + 2 * k) 0) * (2 * n) + t) false)
+  (hR₁ : ∀ q (i : Fin C.gates.length) (a : Fin (C.gates[i.val]).args.length → Bool)
+      (t : Fin n),
+    ((seed q).2.1 i a).2 t = q.getD (2 * S * n + (4 * i +
+      (List.ofFn a).foldr (fun b k => b.toNat + 2 * k) 0) * (2 * n) + n + t) false)
+  (hP : ∀ q (i : Fin C.gates.length) (a : Fin (C.gates[i.val]).args.length → Bool)
+      (t : Fin (C.gates[i.val]).args.length),
+    (seed q).2.2 i a t = xor (a t) (q.getD (10 * S * n + 2 * i + t) false))
+
+include hK hR₀ hR₁ hP
+
+/-- A seed with the four addressed readers is exactly the extracted actual
+key/nonce tables together with the permutation derived from retained shifts.
+No actual sampler is defined here. [PS10, Theorem 163.7]
+`seed (List.ofFn r) = ((equiv C S n hA hS r).2.1, (equiv C S n hA hS r).2.2, permutation C S n hA hS
+(equiv C S n hA hS r).1)`.
+
+**Proof sketch.** Compare keys coordinate by coordinate using the key-address
+reader. Compare each nonce pair using the two half-address readers. Finally,
+compare assignment permutations pointwise with the retained-shift identity.
+These equalities determine the three components of the existing seed. -/
+theorem seed_eq (r : Fin (10 * S * n + 2 * S) → Bool) :
+    seed (List.ofFn r) =
+      ((equiv C S n hA hS r).2.1, (equiv C S n hA hS r).2.2,
+        permutation C S n hA hS (equiv C S n hA hS r).1) := by
+  apply Prod.ext
+  · funext w b t
+    rw [hK, equiv_key]
+  · apply Prod.ext
+    · funext i a
+      apply Prod.ext
+      · funext t
+        rw [hR₀, equiv_nonce_first]
+      · funext t
+        rw [hR₁, equiv_nonce_second]
+    · funext i
+      apply Equiv.ext
+      intro a
+      exact seed_perm C S n hA hS seed hP r i a
+
+/-- On the inverse tape encoded by a retained parameter and actual value table,
+the supplied seed has exactly those keys/nonces and the parameter's derived
+permutation. This is the concrete sampler identification used by coordinate fill.
+[PS10, Theorem 163.7]
+`seed (List.ofFn ((equiv C S n hA hS).symm (p, d))) = (d.1, d.2, permutation C S n hA hS p)`. -/
+theorem seed_on_inverse (p : Rest C S n hA hS)
+    (d : (Fin C.size → Bool → Fin n → Bool) ×
+      ((i : Fin C.gates.length) → (Fin (C.gates[i.val]).args.length → Bool) →
+        (Fin n → Bool) × (Fin n → Bool))) :
+    seed (List.ofFn ((equiv C S n hA hS).symm (p, d))) =
+      (d.1, d.2, permutation C S n hA hS p) := by
+  have h := seed_eq C S n hA hS seed hK hR₀ hR₁ hP ((equiv C S n hA hS).symm (p, d))
+  rw [Equiv.apply_symm_apply] at h
+  exact h
+
+end Seed
+
+/-- A padded key coordinate at vertex `w ≥ C.size` is outside the actual-key
+embedding image and therefore belongs to the retained parameter. -/
+theorem key_not_mem_range {m : ℕ} (C : DAGCircuit m) (S : ℕ) (hS : C.size ≤ S)
+    (w : Fin S) (b : Bool) (hw : C.size ≤ (w : ℕ)) :
+    (b, w) ∉ Set.range (keyEmbedding C S hS) := by
+  rintro ⟨⟨w', b'⟩, h⟩
+  have hval : (w' : ℕ) = (w : ℕ) := by
+    simpa only [keyEmbedding_eq, Fin.coe_castLE] using
+      congrArg (fun x : Bool × Fin S => (x.2 : ℕ)) h
+  have := w'.isLt
+  omega
+
+/-- A row at a padded gate index beyond the actual gate list is outside the
+actual-row embedding image and remains in the retained parameter.
+`(i, a) ∉ Set.range (rowEmbedding C S hA hS)`. -/
+theorem row_not_mem_range_of_gate {m : ℕ} (C : DAGCircuit m) (S : ℕ)
+    (hA : ∀ i : Fin C.gates.length, (C.gates[i.val]).args.length ≤ 2) (hS : C.size ≤ S)
+    (i : Fin S) (a : Fin 4) (hi : C.gates.length ≤ (i : ℕ)) :
+    (i, a) ∉ Set.range (rowEmbedding C S hA hS) := by
+  rintro ⟨⟨j, b⟩, h⟩
+  have hval : (j : ℕ) = (i : ℕ) := by
+    simpa only [rowEmbedding_eq C S hA hS (gateBound C S hS), Fin.coe_castLE] using
+      congrArg (fun x : Fin S × Fin 4 => (x.1 : ℕ)) h
+  have := j.isLt
+  omega
+
+/-- At a real gate, every row code at least `2^arity` is outside the actual-row
+image. Thus arity zero retains codes one through three, and arity one retains
+two and three in the unchanged parameter.
+`(Fin.castLE hG i, code) ∉ Set.range (rowEmbedding C S hA hS)`.
+
+**Proof sketch.** Equality with an embedded row first identifies its gate index.
+At that same gate, the published assignment-code bound puts every embedded code
+strictly below `2^arity`, contradicting the given unused-code bound. -/
+theorem row_not_mem_range_of_code {m : ℕ} (C : DAGCircuit m) (S : ℕ)
+    (hA : ∀ i : Fin C.gates.length, (C.gates[i.val]).args.length ≤ 2) (hS : C.size ≤ S)
+    (hG : C.gates.length ≤ S) (i : Fin C.gates.length) (code : Fin 4)
+    (hc : 2 ^ (C.gates[i.val]).args.length ≤ (code : ℕ)) :
+    (Fin.castLE hG i, code) ∉ Set.range (rowEmbedding C S hA hS) := by
+  rintro ⟨⟨j, a⟩, h⟩
+  have hji : j = i := by
+    apply Fin.ext
+    simpa only [rowEmbedding_eq C S hA hS hG, Fin.coe_castLE] using
+      congrArg (fun x : Fin S × Fin 4 => (x.1 : ℕ)) h
+  cases hji
+  have hcode :
+      (CoinSeedRelabel.assignmentEmbedding (C.gates[i.val]).args.length (hA i) a : ℕ) =
+        (code : ℕ) := by
+    simpa only [rowEmbedding_eq C S hA hS hG] using
+      congrArg (fun x : Fin S × Fin 4 => (x.2 : ℕ)) h
+  have hlt := CoinSeedRelabel.assignmentEmbedding_lt_pow
+    (C.gates[i.val]).args.length (hA i) a
+  omega
+
+end TCSlib.Cryptography.SecureComputation.YaoHybridProof.TapeValues
diff --git a/TCSlib/Cryptography/SecureComputation/Yao/HybridProof/TwoMoveViews.lean b/TCSlib/Cryptography/SecureComputation/Yao/HybridProof/TwoMoveViews.lean
